- Finding 4 (the gating clause of `stuck_set` is a liveness premise on the bodies: an ungated body may still condition a send on data received elsewhere, and every Job must run): **accepted**, A-P1 and A-P4 amended: the theorem assumes each Job performs the enabled operations of its ungated roles and schedulability (P-18) makes every Job run; the gate obligation proves gating only where declared.
- Findings 5 and 8 (the set's representation; the sender clause's quantification): **confirmed as intended**: `set` is a Boolean vector indexed by role, so partial sets are covered; the sender clause quantifies over every receive transition of a blocked member.
- Finding 6 (a role behind a wrong label at the head of its channel is not a deadlock by R41.3's wording, and with three or more roles could starve while others loop): **accepted as a stated non-goal**: the explorer rejects such configurations as unspecified receptions, the certificate does not; the multiparty theorem's merge is what excludes them (section 13).
- Finding 7 (finished peers, initial configuration, refused sends): no issue, as found.
- Finding 10 (`gconv` takes a choice's receiver from its first branch): **accepted as a reliance on `well_formed`**, which has already rejected a branch to another receiver when the conversion runs; stated in A-P9.
- Finding 11 (one capacity per Endpoint, k not an `ensures`): the declaration gives one capacity per Endpoint for every directed channel (R38.1's "capacity of each directed channel" is that one value); **k is an `ensures` of the verified exploration since 2026-11-09** (the universal bound, attained by a listed configuration, checked equal to the explorer's).
- Finding 13: no issue.
- Finding 14(f) (a dropped role state, R41.2, leaves a role blocked with no response): **accepted and closed the next day (2026-11-09)**: a Protocol violation now names its Endpoint and role (`rsk::report_protocol_violation(id, role)`, the SVC argument `2 | e << 8 | role << 16`, checked against the executing Task) and the Kernel re-initializes the Endpoint after recording it (`endpoint_reinit_one`, as for a rejected message), so every role starts the Protocol over and A-P6 covers the configuration; the QEMU demo's two injected violations leave its counters unchanged (both fall in `control`'s last Job).
- Finding 15 (the multiparty merge under recursion and the projection of `Rec` onto an uninvolved role): **accepted as the known pitfalls of the open theorem** (Scalas and Yoshida), recorded with task 21.7.

**The gating exercised (2026-11-08, evening).** The QEMU configuration and the plain measurement configuration declare, beside `pingpong` (`control` to `log`), two more Endpoints under the same Protocol: `relay: protocol PingPong { ctl => part1.log after = pingpong, sink => part2.filter validate = { Req = crate::validate_req } ops = 2 }` and `report: protocol PingPong { ctl => part2.filter after = relay, sink => part0.control validate = { Req = crate::validate_rep } ops = 2 }` (the validation functions where R42.4 demands them, the requests crossing to a higher Criticality_Level): three Protocol Endpoints, two gating edges, ranks 0, 1, 2 in the summary; `log` holds two Protocol roles (pingpong's sink, relay's ctl), `filter` two (relay's sink, report's ctl), `control` two (pingpong's ctl, report's sink). `log` steps `relay` only in a Job whose `pingpong` receive completed, `filter` steps `report` only after its `relay` receive, each through the gate above. QEMU (200 ms): RSK-OK with `relay_reqs 2, relay_rounds 2, report_reqs 1, report_rounds 1` (rounds complete at 40 and 120 ms on `relay`, 100 ms on `report`; `part1`'s stop at 170 ms re-initializes `relay`), every earlier counter unchanged (`pingpong_reps 4, pingpong_reqs 5, pingpong_rejected 1, pingpong_peer 1, telemetry_peer 1`, two Protocol violations), the safe state requiring at least one round on each new Endpoint. One tuning: the QEMU configuration's `sensor` Budget 8.75 to 8.70 ms, since `control`'s `report` sink adds two operations to its O_i and its placeholder slack of 50 cycles was gone (the measured Job is 6.4 ms; the HIL configurations keep their Budgets). On the rig (the plain measurement configuration, 180 s, 2026-11-08 evening): RSK-OK with 4,500 `pingpong` rounds, 2,250 `relay` rounds, and 1,125 `report` rounds (every `log` Job that received a request drove `relay`, every `filter` Job that received one drove `report`), no rejection and no peer-state error; the measured analysis PASS at 97 % utilization with `log` at 39.8 ms of its 40 ms deadline (observed maximum 4.2 ms; the two new roles' sites and operations consumed most of its 1.0 ms of slack, and the next campaign's parameters could tip it, which the Analyzer would then report as the plan intends); Δ_send 2,862 and Δ_recv 1,027 cycles with the margin (2,708 and 914 in the morning's run: the new roles' payloads and the gate are in the Rust half of the SVC), L_kernel 20,765 (17,600 in the morning, 20,534 the day before: run-to-run spread). The record is refreshed; the hour-long records stay stale (R33.4).

**Evidence.** The Property 20 PBT runs the certificate on every generated Protocol (300 cases per job); `tests/r41_3_search.rs` searches a broader space (up to four roles, choices at any role, nested recursions, three-way choices) for a Protocol the structural checks, the projection, and the exploration accept but the certificate refuses, and found none: 100,000 generated types on 2026-11-08 at capacity 2, of which 38,536 were accepted (3,984 with two roles, 18,174 with three, 16,378 with four) and certified, 52,766 rejected under R38.5 and 8,402 under R41.1, none refused by the certificate; the job runs 40 cases of it. `tests/r41_3_enumeration.rs` replaces the random search with a bounded exhaustive enumeration of small Protocols over three roles, three labels, and capacity 2, in three tiers, each complete within its bound: (1) no recursion, depth 2, two-way choices at any role to any receiver: 109,891 types; (2) no recursion, depth 1, two- and three-way choices: 91; (3) one top-level recursion with `continue` as an extra terminator, body depth 2, two-way choices: 1,195,742; in all 1,305,724 types (about 90 s in the job): 86,004 accepted and certified (71,748 with two roles, 14,256 with three), 1,128,816 rejected by the merge (every one "cannot tell the branches apart", R38.5), 90,900 by the bound (R41.1, all in the recursive tier), 4 with fewer than two roles, and none refused by the certificate. Depth 3 without recursion would be about 4 × 10^11 types and is out of reach; the unit tests of `progress` (a certified loop, a rootless or unclosed list, an over-capacity channel, two roles awaiting each other, a waiting pair beside a live pair); the Generator test `r41_3_gating_is_declared_acyclic_and_ranked` (two independent Protocol Endpoints on the same Tasks accepted at rank 0, an acyclic gating ranked 0 and 1, a cycle through two Tasks refused naming both Endpoints, `after` naming itself, an unknown name, or a plain Endpoint refused); the QEMU demo's rejection path unchanged in its observable counts (one rejection, one peer-state error per Endpoint, `part1` stopped). Proof mutations (9.2): `progress-certificate-without-root`, `progress-empty-waiting-set`, `progress-terminal-is-active`, `compose-ranked-not-strict`, `endpoint-reinit-one-role-only`, `channel-push-wrong-slot`, each killed.

**The general theorem, two-role fragment (`rsk-protocol::theory`, Verus, 135 items, 2026-11-08).** The Protocol syntax as a specification datatype `G` (`End`; `Comm` from one role to another with a list of labelled branches, a message being a one-branch `Comm`; `Rec` and `Var` in de Bruijn form), the structural acceptance condition `wf2(g)` (closed; every recursion variable guarded by a message between its own recursion and itself, the environment of guard flags one per enclosing recursion; every communication between the two distinct roles 0 and 1 with at least one branch and pairwise distinct labels: what `well_formed` and the parser accept for two roles, the merge being trivial since both roles take part in every communication; **the first version demanded a message since the nearest recursion, stricter than the checker, and the job's run of `tests/r41_3_search.rs` found `rec X { D(u32): d -> b; rec Y { continue X } }`, accepted by the checker and refused by the verified acceptance, on 2026-11-08 at night: the specification was corrected to the standard rule, the substitution lemma re-proven with the environment, and the search and the enumeration agree again on 100,000 and 1,305,724 types**), the projection `proj(g, r)` (R38.3), substitution and head normal form (`subst`, `unfold`, `head`, with the lemmas that unfolding a closed well-formed recursion keeps well-formedness and removes one leading recursion), and a semantics on local types and FIFO channels derived from the syntax alone (`LC`: the two roles' local types and the two channels; `lstep`; `lrun`; `lreachable`; `lblocked`, `lterminal`, `ldeadlocked`, the two-role instance of the waiting set). The theorem:

- `pub proof fn theorem_two_role_progress(g: G) requires wf2(g) ensures forall|c: LC| lreachable(g, c) ==> !ldeadlocked(c)`: every Protocol the structural acceptance conditions admit has progress in every run, with no enumeration. Its proof is the invariant `inv`: in every reachable configuration one role leads; there is a well-formed global type `g0` at the lagger's position, the labels in transit from the leader are a path `g0` follows (`follow`), the leader's type is the head of the projection of the type reached, and the other channel is empty; `lemma_init`, `lemma_step_inv` (the leader sends and goes one message further; the lagger sends only with nothing in transit, and the roles swap; the lagger receives the oldest label and `g0` advances; the leader cannot receive, its channel being empty), and `lemma_no_deadlock` (with labels in transit the lagger can receive the oldest; with none, both stand at `g0`, whose head is `End` or a communication whose sender can send).
- The hypothesis is established by the checker: `pub fn accept_two_role(g: &GE) -> (r: bool) ensures r ==> wf2(g@), r ==> forall|c| lreachable(g@, c) ==> !ldeadlocked(c)` is verified exec code (`wf2_exec`, sound; complete for recursions nested below 2^20) that `check_protocol` runs on every two-role Protocol after its structural checks (a refusal would be a discrepancy and names R41.3; none in the property, search, and enumeration tests), on the parsed type's view, which the verified `theory::to_theory` computes (2026-11-08, later the same day): the parser's `Global` is made visible to the specifications (`external_type_specification`), the conversion is specified as `gconv` over it (roles numbered by their position among the Endpoint's roles, labels by first appearance in the Protocol, `continue` to de Bruijn by the innermost binder, a choice's receiver that of its first message branch), the label list is proven distinct and complete (so the numbering is injective), and `to_theory` ensures its result's view is `gconv` of the parsed type; so between the source text and the theorem the only unverified step is the parser (`parse.rs`, plain Rust, which `rsk-gen` tests and Property 20 exercise). So for a two-role Endpoint the Generator accepts, progress follows from the theorem; the per-Protocol certificate (`progress::certify`) is kept as an independent cross-check, and it is what still establishes the capacity bound (R41.1), a semantic condition the theorem does not need (the model's sends never meet a full channel; the certificate shows the real channels never fill). Proof mutations `theory-unguarded-variable` and `theory-deadlock-without-waiter`.
- The bridge to the automaton: `pub proof fn theorem_two_role_automaton(s: System, g: G, tb: Seq<Seq<L>>) requires wf2(g), flattens(s, g, tb) ensures forall|c: Cfg| reachable(s, c) ==> !deadlocked(s, c)`, where `flattens` says the automaton `s` is the flattening of `g` through state tables `tb` (entry 0 the head of the projection, every entry head normal between the two roles, the transitions exactly the entries' branches each leading to the head of its continuation): every automaton run has a local run beside it (`lemma_bridge_run`, `corresponds`) and a waiting set of the automaton is a waiting set of the local types (`lemma_bridge_deadlock`). This is the hypothesis `compose::theorem_system_r41_3` takes per Endpoint, so for two-role Endpoints the whole chain, from the declaration's syntax to the system's progress under declared gating, is proven without a certificate; what remains glue is that `explore::certify`'s flattening satisfies `flattens` (plain Rust) and `to_theory`. Mutations `theory-bridge-receive-entry` and, for the conversion, `theory-rec-index-not-de-bruijn`.
- The verified flattening and exploration (2026-11-09). `theory::flatten_two(g: &GE, capacity) -> Option<(System, Vec<LE>, Vec<LE>)> ensures Some ==> flattens(s, g@, tables)`: executable local types with a view to `L`, the executable projection, substitution, and head normal form (each `ensures` its specification, the substitution and head form refusing with `None` on an index overflow rather than computing a wrong term), and the per-role state table built by a worklist over head-normal types with structural deduplication (`intern`, bounded by 4,096 states), every entry admissible and every branch a transition to the head of its continuation. `progress::explore_system(s) -> Option<(Vec<Config>, usize)> ensures Some ==> certificate(s, list) && (forall reachable c: !deadlocked && within_capacity) && k <= capacity && every listed channel content at most k && some listed content equal to k`: the breadth-first exploration of the automaton from the initial configuration (checked like every other), bounded by 200,000 configurations, which makes R41.1's bound k an `ensures` (the universal bound, attained; the review's finding 11). `check_protocol` runs both on every two-role Protocol after the structural checks and the untrusted explorer, refuses on any `None`, requires the verified k to equal the explorer's, and still runs `certify` on the verified list as the cross-check. Between the source text and the system theorem the only unverified step is now the parser; findings 1 and 2 of the review are closed for two roles (the automaton the certificate speaks of is proven to be the flattening of the projection of the parsed syntax). **The chain found a defect in the Protocol_Checker the same night:** the search's first run with the verified exploration refused 437 of 100,000 Protocols the explorer accepted; each contained a recursion in whose body a role takes no part, such as `rec X { A(): d -> a; rec Y { continue X } }`, which the checker's projection turned into `end` for that role (the standard pruning of a vacuous recursion, applied also when its body is a `continue` of an enclosing recursion), so an unbounded stream was accepted with k = 1 and a projection that stops after one message. The projection is corrected (`check::collapse_vacuous`: the vacuous binder is removed, a `continue` of an enclosing recursion stands for the whole, a `continue` of the vacuous recursion itself is `end`), the explorer now refuses the stream under R41.1 as the verified exploration does, and the search (100,000) and the enumeration (1,305,724) agree again. Mutations `theory-flatten-any-peer` and `explore-without-deadlock-check`.
- (from the independent review of 2026-11-08, 5.15): the role modules against the projection are unverified glue (finding 12), and the flattening for three or four roles (`explore::certify`; for two roles it is verified since 2026-11-09, findings 1 and 2 closed); a wrong label at the head of a channel with three or more roles is a stated non-goal (finding 6); (finding 14(f) closed 2026-11-09: a Protocol violation re-initializes its Endpoint);
- Task 21.7: the multiparty progress theorem with the full merge, **proven 2026-11-09** (`rsk-protocol::multiparty::theorem_multiparty_progress`, below). An Endpoint of three or four roles is now covered by the theorem over the acceptance conditions `acceptn` of the syntax; the R38.1 refusal applies above four roles. What remains glue for three or four roles is named in section 13: the Protocol_Checker's plain-Rust acceptance (`well_formed`, `project`) against `acceptn`, and `explore::certify`'s flattening against the theorem's semantics (the two-role chain is verified end to end).

**Multiparty Endpoints (task 21.7, 2026-11-08, night).** The QEMU configuration and the plain measurement configuration declare a three-role Protocol beside the two-role ones: `Triangle` (`ctl` requests, `mid` forwards, `sink` acknowledges; a stop branch) on `triangle: protocol Triangle { ctl => part0.control validate = { Ack = .. }, mid => part1.log ops = 2, sink => part2.filter validate = { Fwd = .. } ops = 2 }`, which the checker bounds at k = 1 on each of its three channels and certifies over 10 configurations (`sink`'s projection is the merge of the two branches). QEMU (200 ms): RSK-OK with 3 triangle rounds beside every earlier counter; under the flood variant 6 rounds. The rig (the plain measurement configuration, 180 s, 2026-11-08 night, after the retune of `sensor`'s Budget 8.25 to 8.0 ms that the two extra operations of `log` required, its measured Job 6.4 ms, 7.9 with the margin): RSK-OK with 3,600 triangle rounds, every pulse answered, Δ_send 2,927 and Δ_recv 1,088 cycles with the margin over the three payload sizes, L_kernel 20,498; the first run's loopback had lost 4.7 s of capture around 73.85 s (the target had counted 9,436 pulses of 10,000 and the Kernel recorded nothing), the rerun clean; the measured analysis of the record is judged by the job (forecast: PASS at 97 %, `log` 267 µs of slack). The Kernel, the `rsk` API, the Generator, and the Config_Checker take Protocol-governed Endpoints of two to four roles (`MAX_ROLES`): one channel per ordered pair of distinct roles (`ArchState::fifos`, channel `from * MAX_ROLES + to` of the Endpoint; the storage dense, `roles * roles * capacity * payload`), the SVC argument naming the operating role and its peer (`e | role << 8 | peer << 12`), the arrival release of the addressed role (R40.3), a rejected message's report naming the sending role (`1 | e << 8 | role << 16 | from << 20`) so that the sender's Partition takes the fault (R42.4) and the whole Endpoint re-initializes (A-P7), a stop or restart re-initializing every role (R14.10, R42.3); the generated role modules address each operation to the projection's peer (`send_to`, `recv_from`); the Endpoint image carries the role count and `MAX_ROLES` slots per array, which the Config_Checker recomputes; the R42.4 rule validates each received label against the level of the role that sends it; the plain form stays two-role. The Kernel's cores re-verified (Verus, `entry.rs`); the demo and its tests unchanged in behaviour under the new addressing (QEMU RSK-OK). The acceptance conditions over the syntax for `n` roles are specifications in `theory.rs`: `wfn` (closed, guarded, every communication between two distinct roles below `n`, non-empty distinct branches), `merge` (the full merge: equal types, or external choices from one role merged branch by branch), `projn` (projection with the merge for a role a communication does not involve), `acceptn(g, n)`, and the `n`-role semantics `NC`, `nstep`, `nreachable`, `ndeadlocked` (R41.3's waiting set over `n` roles). **The multiparty progress theorem was proven on 2026-11-09** (next paragraph); until then an Endpoint of three or four roles rested on the per-Protocol certificate alone.

**The multiparty progress theorem (`rsk-protocol::multiparty`, Verus, 198 items, 2026-11-09).** (Proven a second time on 2026-11-15, in Lean, on the acceptance check as compiled: 5.20.) `theorem_multiparty_progress(g, n)`: `acceptn(g, n) ==> forall c: nreachable(g, n, c) ==> !ndeadlocked(c, n)`, with `acceptn` (closed, guarded, every communication between two distinct roles below `n` with at least one branch and distinct labels, projectable onto every role with the full merge), the `n`-role semantics `nstep`/`nreachable`, and the waiting set `ndeadlocked` exactly as stated in `theory.rs` on 2026-11-08 (the review's statements, 5.15; nothing in them changed). The proof: a reachable configuration is described by a *runtime global type* `R`, the syntax with one more form `Sent` (a communication whose message is in transit: the sender has moved on, the receiver has not); its queue per ordered pair is the sequence of in-transit labels along the first branches, and the structure `rgood` requires no message in transit inside a recursion (a step unfolds every recursion above it), none from the sender of a pending communication below it (a role's sends are in order), and every branch of a pending choice agreeing on its queues (a step rewrites every branch alike). A step of the configuration is mirrored by `rstep_send`/`rstep_recv`, which rewrite the acting role's first pending node in every branch of every pending choice it is not part of, after head-normalizing the path (`rhead`, by `rlead_recs` unfoldings). **The relation between a role and the runtime type is coinductive, not a projection:** after a choice is taken the runtime type keeps the taken branch while a bystander's type stays the merge, and after further steps the branches of a still-pending choice need not merge any more (a taken choice below leaves different send continuations in two branches: the merged projection of a reachable runtime type is undefined in general, a fact found while attempting the projection-based invariant), so the invariant relates `c.l[u]` to `R` by `fits`: a set of pairs closed under `consistent` (`node_ok`: the runtime type well formed; a role with an action reached on every path, which bounds the search for its pending node; at the head, the role's next action is the head's with the continuation pairs in the set, a sender's labels exactly the communication's, a receiver's a superset; at a choice the role is not part of, the same local type paired with every branch; at a message in transit to the role, its label in the receive). Initially the set is "the role's head refines the head of the merged projection" (`rel0`; `refines`: more labels in external choices; the merge refines each of its arguments, refinement survives substitution and head normalization), consistent by `lemma_rel0_consistent`; a step maps it to `rel_send`/`rel_recv` (every pair, and every stepped pair with the actor's continuation), consistent by `lemma_rel_send_consistent`/`lemma_rel_recv_consistent`, the actor's reaching re-established from the children pairs (`lemma_send_reaches_actor`). The existence of the mirrored step is by induction on the greatest depth of the role's pending node over the paths (`sdepth`, which head normalization does not increase: `lemma_rhead_reaches`), the receive case using the sender order and the queue agreement (`lemma_recv_exists`). The invariant `minv` (well formed, good, every role fits, every channel equals its queue) holds initially (`lemma_init`, with `rproj(emb(g), u) == projn(g, u)` and `rwf(emb(g)) <==> wfn(g)`) and is kept by every step (`lemma_step`). No waiting set exists under it (`lemma_no_deadlock`): an awaiting member `r` is reached on every path, so it has a first pending node along the first branches at depth `fdepth(x, r)`; the role at the head of the runtime type can move (the sender of a pending communication, whose head is a send with exactly its labels; the receiver of a message in transit, whose channel's oldest label is that message), it is not a member (a member is awaiting or finished), and the configuration after its move, which `lemma_send_minv`/`lemma_recv_minv` keep in the invariant, has the same waiting set (`lemma_status_same`: nothing a member awaits changed) and the member's node one shallower (`lemma_blocked_depth`, induction on the depth). Two definitional points: the head the invariant uses is `mhead`, the semantics' `lhead` for every type whose spine ends in an action or an end, and the one canonical form `rec X { continue X }` for a variable-spined type (the projection of a recursion a role takes no part in, which `merge` admits and the Protocol_Checker prints as `end`: in the semantics such a role is neither awaiting nor finished nor able to move, under either head, `lemma_mhead_lhead`; the Protocol_Checker's `collapse_vacuous` and the theory's `projn` differ exactly there, both leaving the role without an action); and the empty branch list, which well-formedness excludes, reaches no role. Mutations `multiparty-sender-order-dropped`, `multiparty-reach-not-required`, `multiparty-send-labels-superset`. The glue that remains for three or four roles (section 13): the checker's plain-Rust acceptance against `acceptn` (for two roles `accept_two_role` is verified) and `explore::certify`'s flattening against the theorem's semantics (for two roles `flatten_two`).

**The glue for three or four roles closed (2026-11-09, later the same day).** (1) **The verified acceptance for `n` roles**: `multiparty::acceptn_exec(g, n)` decides `acceptn` on the executable global type (`wfn_exec` with the role bound; `merge_exec`, `projn_exec` with the full merge; `r ==> acceptn(g@, n)`, and `!r ==> !acceptn(g@, n)` unless the type nests more than `usize::MAX - 2` recursions, where the executable shift refuses rather than overflows), and `accept_multiparty` concludes the theorem's conclusion; `check_protocol` runs it on every Protocol of three or more roles after the structural checks and reports a refusal as a discrepancy (R41.3). **Its first run disagreed with the checker**, on Property 20's `A(): c -> a; choice at a { L(): a -> b; A(): b -> a; end } or { R(): a -> b; rec X { Req(u8): a -> b; Rep(u8): b -> a; end } }`: for `c` the checker collapses the recursion it takes no part in (`collapse_vacuous`, `end`) while the theory's `projn` kept `rec X. end`, and `merge(end, rec X. end)` is undefined. The theory's rule is now the checker's (`theory::close_rec`: a body projection with no action makes the recursion vacuous, its `continue` an end and an enclosing `continue` the whole; a body projection using the variable keeps the recursion; a body projection not using it drops the binder, which is the one case where the two rules would still differ, so **R38.5 now refuses an unused recursion**, `rec X` with no `continue X`, a degenerate form no Protocol needs). The multiparty proof was re-established on the new rule: the rule commutes with the substitution of a closed normal type (`lemma_close_rec_subst`, through `lshift`, the removal of an unused binder), the projection of a well-formed runtime type is normal (no recursion of a body without action) and closed (`lemma_rproj_normal`), and `lemma_mhead_rproj` takes the three cases. The search (100,000), Property 20, and the enumeration (1,305,724, the documented counts unchanged) agree. (2) **The verified flattening for `n` roles**: `multiparty::flatten_n(g, n, capacity)` builds the roles' state tables and the automaton, `ensures flattens_n(s, g@, n, tables)` (the `n`-role `flattens`: entry 0 the head of the projection, every entry admissible with a peer among the other roles, the transitions exactly the entries' branches addressed to the entry's peer), and `theorem_multiparty_automaton(s, g, n, tb)`: `acceptn(g, n) && flattens_n(s, g, n, tb) ==> forall c. reachable(s, c) ==> !deadlocked(s, c)`, by the correspondence `corresponds_n` (each role's head the table entry of its state, each pair's channel the automaton's) carried along every run and the waiting set carried back (`lemma_nbridge_status`: an awaiting automaton role awaits in the configuration on the same sender, a finished one has finished). `check_protocol` runs `flatten_n` and `explore_system` on every Protocol of three or more roles as it runs `flatten_two` for two, with the same bound cross-check and `certify` on the verified list. **So for every role count the chain from the parsed syntax to the system theorem is verified except the parser.** Mutations `multiparty-close-rec-keeps-rec`, `multiparty-acceptn-exec-skips-projection`, `multiparty-flatten-peer-zero`, `multiparty-bridge-channels-unlinked`. (3) **`log` has an App_Prover member** (`proofs/creusot/part1`, the QEMU configuration's features; Creusot: Proved, 47 files): the one gated Job whose body was covered by QEMU alone now proves its A-P8 obligation with its body, like `control` and `filter`; its two loops carry the lint's bounds and its counters saturate (Creusot does not lower a byte-string literal, so the debug tags are byte arrays, as in the other Partitions). The crate: 481 items verified.

**No unexpected reception (2026-11-09, evening).** Both progress theorems now have a companion: in every reachable configuration, the oldest label of a channel a role awaits on is one the role accepts. Two roles: `theory::lreception_ok(c)` (a role whose head is a receive and whose channel from the other role is not empty finds the oldest label in its branches), `theorem_two_role_reception(g)`: `wf2(g) ==> forall c. lreachable(g, c) ==> lreception_ok(c)`, from the invariant (only the lagging role's channel can be non-empty, and the lagger's receive is the projection of the communication at `g0`'s head). `n` roles: `multiparty::reception_ok(c, n)`, `theorem_multiparty_reception(g, n)`: `acceptn(g, n) ==> forall c. nreachable(g, n, c) ==> reception_ok(c, n)`, from `lemma_head_accepted` (the argument of the receive step's existence, concluding the lookup: on every path the role's first pending node is the message in transit whose label heads the channel, by the sender order and the queue agreement, and the relation says the role accepts that label). Mutations `theory-inv-lagger-channel-nonempty`, `multiparty-sent-label-unchecked`.

**The Kernel link and the labels (A-P2's message half, 2026-11-09, evening).** The link of goal 3 covered the per-pair channels of three- and four-role Endpoints already: `channel_push` and `channel_pop` act on the channel `channel_index(e, from, to)` the cores compute for the ordered pair, and their postconditions are per channel. What the cores did not state was the message: the copy was an action the hardware model ignored. Now the model carries `store`, the first word of every message copied into the Endpoint storage (word address to word), written by the one architecture operation `copy_in` (the copy into a slot, returning the first word now there) and read by `copy_out` (the copy out of a slot, returning the stored first word; the storage is Kernel-owned, R16.1, so `copy_in` is its only writer: the architecture's trusted contract, with `apply`). `channel_labels(h, base, cfg, d, fifo)` is a channel's label sequence, the stored first word of each occupied slot, oldest first, and the cores ensure (`send_labels_ok`, `recv_labels_ok`, over the Endpoint table as the uninterpreted specification `spec_endpoint_of`, `spec_storage_base_of` of the generated constants): an accepted send appends the sent message's first word to its channel's label sequence (the next free slot is none of the occupied ones, `lemma_next_slot_fresh`; distinct slots have distinct addresses, `lemma_slot_addr_distinct`), and an accepted receive removes the oldest label and delivers that word first (the receive core returns it as a ghost value). So the Kernel's channel of R41.3 is now a sequence of labels, not of slots, and A-P2's message half reduces to the role API placing the label in the message's first word (`w[0] = LABEL_X`, generated; the Creusot-compiled role modules carry the bodies' proofs, and the word is set in every generated send), with the storage's integrity against other writers as the hardware assumption. Kernel: 209 items verified. Mutations `kernel-recv-delivered-zero`, `kernel-send-labels-stale-slot`; the register's `channel-push-wrong-slot` re-pointed to the extended postcondition.

**The role API's half of A-P2 (2026-11-09, night).** The `rsk` crate gives the generated role modules two word-level operations with trusted contracts under Creusot: `Endpoint<[u32; W], W>::send_words(peer, &w)` ensures, on `Ok`, the opaque predicate `rsk::channel::sent(id, role, peer, w[0])` (the Kernel's send on that channel accepted a message whose first word is `w[0]`), and `recv_words(peer)` ensures, on `Ok(Some(w))`, `rsk::channel::received(id, peer, role, w[0])`. Every generated `send_<label>` now carries `ensures(Ok ==> sent(ID, ROLE, PEER_<to>, LABEL_<label>))`, which the App_Prover proves from the body (`w[0] = LABEL_<label>` then `send_words`), and every generated `try_recv` carries, per variant, `ensures(Ok(RecvS<i>::<label>(..)) ==> received(ID, PEER_<from>, ROLE, LABEL_<label>))`, proven from the `match w[0]` that selects the variant. The Kernel's `copy_in` assumption (`docs/proof_assumptions.toml`) cites these contracts: the first word it records as the slot's label is, by the App_Prover, the label index of the transition the role took, so the channel's label sequence (`channel_labels`) is the sequence of labels the role modules sent, and the delivered first word the role modules dispatch on is the oldest of them. The predicates are opaque on purpose: they carry no model of the Kernel, only the link between the two proofs' vocabularies, which a reviewer checks against `send_labels_ok` and `recv_labels_ok` (the same `(e, role, peer)` decoding of the SVC argument, `Endpoint::arg`). A-P2 is thereby reduced to the hardware assumption of R16.1 (the storage has no writer but `copy_in`). Proven on the QEMU configuration's three Partitions (every Protocol role of the demo: `pingpong`, `relay`, `report`, `triangle`; 143 files). Mutation `roles-send-label-word-dropped` (the register's first App_Prover mutation, 9.2): the generated send without `w[0] = LABEL`, killed by the copy loop's word-0 invariant and the `sent` postcondition.

**The parser (2026-11-09, evening).** `rsk-protocol::parse_v`: the grammar as a specification, `sp_block(ts, pos)`, the deterministic parse of a token sequence from a position (the type and the position after it), with `sp_parse` for a whole sequence; the executable parser `parse_toks` refining it (`parse_block`, `parse_branches`: `Some(g) ==> sp_block == Some((gview(g), pos'))`, `None ==> sp_block is None`, the AST's view `gview` on `ast::Global`); the printer `print_toks` producing `printed(gview(g))`, the canonical tokens `render` prints; and the round trip, `theorem_round_trip(g)`: `sp_parse(printed(g)) == Some(g)` for every type whose choices have a branch (`gs_ok`), by `lemma_parse_printed` (the grammar reads a printed type back in any context that closes it), with its executable form `round_trip`. `parse::parse` now returns the verified parser's AST; its own parser runs only for the diagnosis when the verified one refuses, and a disagreement is reported. **The lexer is verified too since the night of 2026-11-09** (`rsk-protocol::lex_v`): the tokens a character sequence denotes as a specification, `sp_lex(cs)` (`sp_lex_from`: whitespace skipped, an identifier run `ident_end` classified by `kw` into the keywords `end`, `continue`, `rec`, `choice`, `at`, `or` or an identifier, a payload between balanced parentheses `paren_end` with its text normalized by `normalize`, runs of whitespace to one space and the spaces beside `::` dropped, an empty one `None`, the punctuation, and nothing for a character that fits no token), the executable lexer `lex_chars` refining it (`Some(ts) ==> sp_lex == Some(view(ts))`, `None ==> sp_lex is None`), and the composition `sp_parse_text(cs) = sp_parse(sp_lex(cs))` with `parse_chars` and `parse_text` refining it: `parse::parse` returns the type the text denotes, its own lexer and parser now the diagnosis alone. Identifiers are ASCII (letters, `_`, then digits), as the generated role modules need them. The one step outside the proof is `chars_of`, the standard library's decoding of the `str` into its characters (an `external_body` whose contract is the view, registered in `proof_assumptions.toml`). Mutation `lex-keyword-or-unclassified`. 27 items verified. The conversion `to_theory` has no precondition (every `Global` converts), so the parser's output always satisfies it; the AST's shape the Generator relies on (a choice with a branch) is `gs_ok`, which the grammar guarantees. Mutation `parse-end-position`.

**A-P1, the role modules from the verified tables (2026-11-09, evening).** The Generator's role modules (`rsk-gen::roles::module`) were built from the Protocol_Checker's plain-Rust local types (`roles::states`, a numbering of the syntax). They are now rendered from the verified flattening: `check_protocol` returns, beside the local types, the roles' state tables (`Checked::tables`, the head-normal entries of `flatten_two`/`flatten_n` read into `RoleEntry`: an end, or a send or a receive with its peer and its labels in the projection's order), the automaton's transitions (`Checked::trans`), and the labels in the verified conversion's numbering (`Checked::labels`, the label word); `roles::states_from_tables` turns them into the states the module is rendered from (a state's operations are the entry's labels, each leading to the transition's target; a state is a recursion head when a transition from a later or the same state returns to it; a label's payload type is read from the declaration). By `theorem_two_role_automaton` and `theorem_multiparty_automaton` these states, their permitted operations, and the state after each are the projection's (`flattens`, `flattens_n`: entry 0 the head of the projection, every transition a branch of its entry leading to the head of its continuation), so A-P1's claim about the typestate API is the theorem's for the data; what remains plain Rust is the rendering of that data into text (one method per transition, one marker type per state, `w[0] = LABEL_X` for the label word) and the reading of a table entry (`entry_of`). The two numberings agree on every Protocol of the Profile, as do the recursion-head flags, so no Partition body changed; the flattening's walk of a state's labels, which had built its list tail first (the job's Target build of the evening found it: the states were numbered in reverse branch order), follows the projection's branch order (`labels_of`, its contract order-agnostic, so the proofs stood). Since the night of 2026-11-09 the plain numbering (`roles::states`) no longer exists: `states_for` reads the verified tables alone, and R42.4's lists of the labels a role receives with a payload (`validate.rs`, `received_messages`) come from the tables' `Recv` entries and the verified label numbering, not from the plain projection, which the Generator now renders only in documentation comments and the Task_Model's `projections`. A-P1 remains, narrowed to that rendering and to the review's finding 4 (each Job performs the enabled operations of its ungated roles, a liveness premise on the bodies).

**What is not covered.** Plain Endpoints. Liveness beyond communication: the theorem says a waiting role's message can always be sent by a role not itself waiting or finished, not that it is sent, which is that role's Task being released and completing within its deadline (schedulability, P-18, and the chain bound of R41.4, 5.14).

**A build note.** `rsk-protocol` is a dependency of the `rsk::app!` proc-macro, so vstd with its `std` feature is now in every Flight_Build's host graph. Cargo unifies a root package's features across host and Target when a proc-macro is named as a root of a `--target` build; the job's Target build and Clippy step therefore no longer name `rsk-macros` (it is compiled as `rsk`'s host dependency regardless), which the first job of 2026-11-08 found when vstd was compiled for the Target with `std` (`verify::target_crates`).

### 5.16 The audit of ironoxide-eval-v2 and what it changed (2026-11-13)

An adversarial read-only audit of the tag `ironoxide-eval-v2` (six areas: the assembly stubs, the unsafe hardware layer, the Endpoint code of task 21 in Kernel_Arch and in the logic, the adequacy of the specifications against the requirements, and the trusted base; every finding put to two independent refutation attempts; `docs/paper/audit_ironoxide-eval-v2.md` on the evaluation branch) found the defects below. None was observed on hardware before the audit; finding 5 is visible in a committed record (144 release overlaps against 136 logged misses in the hour-long timing run). Each fix is one commit of the branch `audit-fixes-v2`, re-proven per crate; the mutations are in `docs/proof_mutations.toml`, each killed by a proof failure.

| Finding | Defect | Fix | Evidence added |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | The boot clear of the PPI channels wrote CHENSET: all 32 channels enabled | `NRF_PPI_CHENCLR` is 0x4001_F508; `boot_core` clears before its checks; boot check bit 14 reads PPI.CHEN back as 0 | The Kani case compares against the datasheet address typed independently; host test and `boot_checks_report_each_failed_check` cover bit 14 |
| 1 | EasyDMA windows mapped read-write to their owner, so the mediation of R17.1 could be bypassed with `rsk::mmio::write` | `PartitionLayout::windows_ro`, the view maps such a window without unprivileged write permission; `wf` adds pairwise disjointness of a Partition's own windows; boot check bit 15: every mediated register lies in a read-only window of its Partition; the LAYOUT image and CRC carry the flag | `Layout::lemma_ro_window_not_writable` (no Partition's view grants unprivileged write access to an address of a read-only window); mutations `ro-window-mapped-writable`, `layout-image-window-read-only` (Config_Checker); the isolation matrix's attempt 9 per walker (HIL run pending) |
| 10 | The EasyDMA tables of UARTE, SAADC, PWM and RADIO were wrong for a mediation that writes the byte length as the count | Those classes have no `DmaRegs`: the DD-08 rule rejects an Application that owns one; R17 is claimed for SPIM | Four conformance cases `conf-PR-29-mc-dma-*` |
| 2 | Any Endpoint role could report a rejected message against its peer and impose the peer Partition's fault response | `EndpointCfg::validates` and `ArchState::delivered`: a report is accepted only from the receiving role, over a validated upward Criticality_Level edge, after a delivery; otherwise API misuse of the caller. The Generator rejects a validation function on an edge that is not upward | `rejection_core`'s contract; mutation `rejection-accepted-without-delivery`; conformance case `fi-R42-4-forged-rejection` (QEMU; HIL run pending) |
| 5 | A held Release's deadline event was disarmed when the previous Job ended: the miss of the Job started from it was never logged | `job_return`, `end_top_release`, `end_mit_window` keep the deadline slot while a Release of the Task is held | The contracts state both cases; mutation `job-return-disarms-held-deadline`; acceptance on the rig: logged misses equal release overlaps in the timing record (pending) |
| lead 2.1 | One Kernel entry can end two Jobs outright; Kernel_Arch unwound one level and returned into the second Job's context | `LevelRecord::previous_task`; `epilogue_core` chains to the preempted Job's level when that Job is no longer the executing Job | Kani witness `one_timer_event_ends_two_jobs_when_the_resumed_job_crossed_its_budget_at_a_preemption` (the logic half, on the demo's configuration); the stress campaign (pending) |
| 6 | A second Job-ending Kernel entry in a dispatch stub's entry window overwrote the owed unwind | The first (topmost) level is kept; the deeper one follows by the chain above | Re-proven; the witness build of 5.8a (pending on the rig) |
| 4 | The `SVC_STALE` guard let an ended Job's late SVC run a second time on a context resumed at its own SVC's return | The timer, interrupt and fault shells clear SHCSR.SVCALLPENDED when their entry ended a Job | Shell code (outside `verus!`): inspection; the fault-injection suites (pending) |
| 7 | END_JOB of an interrupt-bound Sporadic_Task's Job left its source masked until the Partition's next restart | `apply_response` ends the MIT window as a Job's return does | Kani harness `end_job_of_a_sporadic_task_ends_its_mit_window`. Contract weakening, approved: the END_JOB case of `response_applied` and the contracts above it state `Timing::unmasked_only` (every instant, the Release history, and `mask_until` kept; a masked source may be unmasked) where they stated equality of the timing state |
| 8, 9, A, D | Kernel entries trusted the process stack pointer: a frame written below a floor with no upper bound; an SVC served on a frame that was never stacked; the stacked PC read through an unchecked pointer | `frame_in_stack`, `psp_in_stack`, `stack_violation_core` (event 27, the Partition's fault response, nothing touched through the pointer) on the dispatch, intake and SVC paths; `plan_frame` takes the region's top; the MemManage stub reads nothing through the frame pointer (`fault_pc`); the stacking fault pending behind an answered SVC is discarded | Mutations `stack-check-lower-bound-dropped`, `stack-check-upper-bound-dropped`, `frame-floor-above-stack-top`; QEMU conformance cases `fi-R16-5-stack-window`, `-unmapped`, `-bottom`; the silicon differential test (SVC at stack bottom + 16) pending. Not expressible as a proof mutation: the order of the check and the first read in the SVC path, which is in the unsafe shim `rsk_svc_rust` (review; the shim calls the check first) |
| 11 | An MIT_Violation past the threshold on a signal raise or a send applied END_JOB to the released Task, not the executing sender, and so restarted the sender's Partition | `mit_violation` takes the offending Task; the three Kernel_Arch call sites pass the executing Job for a raise and a send | Kani harness `mit_violation_of_a_raise_under_end_job_ends_the_raising_job`; mutation `mit-violation-response-for-the-released-task`. The Analyzer's inputs are unchanged: Δ_restart is the measured cost of one restart, and no term counts restarts by cause |
| B | The safe state's loop was `watchdog_feed(); wfi` inside a Kernel-level handler: never woken, the watchdog reset the Target | A busy wait between feeds (`safe_state_wait`) | Kani harness `safe_state_feed_interval_is_inside_the_watchdog_reload`; HIL: ten seconds in a run-time safe state with no watchdog reset (pending) |

Claims corrected in this plan without a code change (findings 12 to 18 and the contested findings E, F, G, I): rows A3, A4, A5, A6 and A8 of section 4; 5.10's atomicity and confinement sentences; A-P6 in 5.15 and row P-22; the assumption counts of sections 1, 4 and 9.1; the Kani registers of 6.2 and 6.3; the premise of the run-level lemmas in 5.1; R18.1's attribution in 11.11; and two (not three) mutation kills by a solver resource limit. `hard_fault`'s HFSR read is in the register list of UJ-001 since the same date (finding 18).

**The review of the fixes (2026-11-13) and `arch_matches`.** The witness of lead 2.1 shows only the logic ending two Jobs; it does not show that Kernel_Arch then unwinds both. The property under lead 2.1, finding 6 and the INVPC defect of 5.8a is one: Kernel_Arch's level records must correspond to the Kernel's preemption stack. Its statement is `entry::arch_matches` (every Job in progress has its active record, for its Task and naming the Task below it; every active record is such a record). What is proven: in the logic, that every transition but a dispatch leaves the stack a prefix of what it was (`Kernel::stack_prefix_of` on all 35 mutating functions, no resource limit raised), and through `KernelOps` (`spec_depth`, `spec_stack`); in Kernel_Arch, the three steps at which the correspondence was broken: a dispatch puts its Job on the stack and writes that Job's record, active and naming the preempted Job (`irq_entry_core`); an owed unwind is consumed or kept, never replaced (`irq_entry_core`); an unwind stops only at the context of the Kernel's executing Job (`epilogue_core`, which also takes a record that disagrees with the stack for a Kernel fault). **The invariant (2026-11-13, later the same day).** `arch_matches` is now established as an invariant of the entry cores, in the form that also holds inside a Kernel entry: `entry::arch_inv(k, state, hop)`. `ArchState` carries a ghost chain (`chain`, `chain_levels`: the Tasks and levels of the Jobs dispatched and not yet unwound, in dispatch order). `chain_ok` states that the chain describes the level records exactly (every entry's record is active, of its Task, names the entry below as the preempted Job, has a non-zero exception number, and sits at the level of its Task's Priority; levels rise strictly along the chain; every active record is a chain entry) and that the Kernel's stack is a prefix of the chain. `arch_inv` adds that the chain is longer than the stack exactly while an unwind is planned (`hop`, the plan handed to the stub) or owed (`pending_unwind`, 5.8a), and that the planned or owed exception number is the topmost record's. Contracts: `boot_core` ensures `arch_inv(.., 0)` (it clears the records, the chain and the owed unwind itself, so nothing is assumed of the initializer); `start_core`, `svc_core`, `fault_core` and `irq_entry_core` require `arch_inv(.., 0)` and ensure `arch_inv` with their plan; `epilogue_core` requires `arch_inv` with the plan it is called for and ensures it with the next one; `timer_core` and `stack_violation_core` ensure `ended_plan`, from which `irq_entry_core` concludes. `lemma_arch_matches`: `arch_inv(.., 0)` with nothing owed implies `arch_matches`; `epilogue_core` ensures `arch_matches` directly when it names no further level. `KernelOps` gained what the proof needs of the logic: `spec_prio` (the Tasks' Priorities, unchanged by every operation), `lemma_stack_rising` (`Sched::inv`: Priorities rise strictly along the stack), `job_info`'s Priority, and the depth facts of `start`, `svc_lock`, `svc_unlock` (unchanged), `job_return` (one less) and a Kernel `fault` (unchanged); all proven for `Bound` from the logic's existing contracts, no logic contract changed. The uniqueness of a level's exception number is not needed: the level is its Task's Priority, and Priorities rise along the stack. Code changes the proof required, none a weakening: the unwind plan after a transition is decided by the stack's depth and names the topmost active record (`end_plan_core`, `plan::top_active`), where it named the level of the Job that was executing; `epilogue_core` unwinds the topmost active record and takes a plan naming another level for a Kernel fault, where it searched for the record by exception number, and clears `pending_unwind`; a dispatch decides everything that can refuse it (floor count, frame) before `Kernel::dispatch`, where a refusal after it left a Job in progress without a record on the way to the safe state, and refuses exception number 0. Mutations, each killed by a proof failure: `unwind-chain-condition-reverted` (assertion), `dispatch-record-drops-previous-task` (precondition of `lemma_push`), `unwind-leaves-record-active` (precondition of `lemma_pop`), `end-plan-ignores-ended-job` (postcondition `ended_plan`), `dispatch-starts-job-before-frame-check` (precondition of `lemma_kernel_entry`), `owed-unwind-not-recorded` (postcondition `arch_inv`). `owed-unwind-overwritten` is retired: with the plan naming the topmost record, the invariant gives that a second owed number equals the first, and Verus verifies that mutant (an equivalent mutant; the `consumed or kept` clause stands). No resource limit was raised for this proof (`chain_ok` is opaque and used through lemmas; `epilogue_core` keeps its limit of 80). **Still assumed, by inspection and test** (the stubs' half is proven since section 5.17)**:** the shells and the assembly stubs are outside Verus, so that each core is entered with the invariant the previous one left, and that a stub calls `epilogue_core` with exactly the plan it was handed and again with each next one until 0, are facts of `arch/cm4/mod.rs` and the stubs (the HIL suites and the Kani harness below exercise them); the proof is of the build with the deferral of 5.8a (`WINDOW_DEFER`), not of the `defer-nofix` witness build. The Kani harness `arch_records_match_the_stack_over_kernel_entries` stays as a bounded cross-check of the executable: the executable form of `arch_matches` after each of five Kernel entries with their unwinds on the demo's configuration, two Budget events in one timer entry among them; it passes on the reworked cores (2026-11-13; with the chain condition of the lead 2.1 fix reverted it failed on the code before the invariant, `active == depth`). Finding 4's shell fix has its Kani case since the same review (`svc_clear_pending_clears_only_the_pending_bit`); the board's fault-injection run remains on the hardware list.

Standing after the audit, with their reasons: the facts of findings 12 to 14 are by inspection and test, not by contract (a contract with a mutant each would close them); FAULTMASK's clearing by an exception return has no Hardware_Model rule (finding 15); R9.1 and R10.1 do not list the exception of R9.9 (a Release whose deadline lies beyond the operating duration is not made; contested finding G), which matters for Release counts: the last Release inside the final deadline window of a run is suppressed; which handler runs first when an SVC's entry cannot stack (the SVC handler or MemManage, at one priority) is the hardware's, unmeasured on the nRF52840 (QEMU 9.2 takes MemManage first); the Kernel answers correctly in both orders.

### 5.9 Core_Subset items without Ferrocene certification

`profile/core_subset.toml` lists six items with `certification = "none"`. Only `core::arch::asm` (stubs, evidence section 6) and `core::panic::PanicInfo` (the panic handler, `location()` only) are used. `NonZeroU8`, `core::array::{from_fn, map}`, the atomics, and `core::hint::spin_loop` are not used by the Kernel crates, and some recorded substitutes do not match the code (for example, the event-log overflow counter is a plain saturating `u32`, not an atomic). Done 2026-10-22: the four unused items were removed from `profile/core_subset.toml` (19 items remain; `cargo xtask profile` regenerated `docs/profile.md`); an item returns with its evidence when first used.

### 5.10 Analyzer evidence beyond the proof (R32; task 15.4)

- **Property 17 as tested.** R32.2 asks that no computed WCRT decrease when a term worsens. The reported value of a Task found not schedulable is the last iterate before the deadline stop (R29.7), a lower bound that is not monotone in the inputs: from base 4 the iterates 4, 6, 8 stop at 8, and from base 5 the iterates 5, 7 stop at 7, with the same interferer (period 4, bound 2, deadline 6). The property therefore holds, and is tested, on fixed points (both results schedulable) and on verdicts (a worsening never makes an unschedulable set schedulable). This is a refinement of the property's wording, not a weakness of the analysis: the lower bound is reported as such (R30.1).
- **The cross-Partition clause of R29.2 is live.** The simulator produced a set in which a Task of a stop-on-overrun Partition overruns inside its Critical_Section and blocks a higher-Priority Task of another Partition for Budget + Δ_detect + Δ_enforce: the Ceiling blocks every lower Priority across Partitions although the Resource stays within one (PR-30). The Analyzer applies the clause; the test harness's own derivation first did not and was corrected. The demo has no instance (every lower non-accessor Task is below sensor's Priority).
- **Overruns and the simulator's claim.** With injected Overruns the bound is checked for the Tasks of Partitions in which no Job overran: R18.7 protects Partitions from one another, and a Partition's own Overrun inside a Critical_Section lengthens the blocking of its own higher Tasks beyond the site's WCET, which the analysis does not claim to cover.
- **Published sets (R32.7, PAR-04 = 3).** Burns and Wellings' response-time example (T = 50, 40, 30; C = 12, 10, 10; R = 10, 20, 52, the lowest Task missing its deadline); the Sha, Klein, and Goodenough three-task example (C/T = 20/100, 40/150, 100/350; completion times 20, 60, 240); the harmonic textbook set C = (1, 2, 3), T = (4, 6, 12) with R = (1, 3, 10), whose attribution (Buttazzo's response-time example) is to be confirmed at review (section 13). Each Budget equals the published execution time; blocking and overheads are zero.
- **Reference implementation (R32.4).** Written separately in the test file with u128 arithmetic and the recurrence evaluated as written; it agrees with the Analyzer on 500 random sets and the demo. It shares the author, not the code (section 8.5).
- **The term derivation proven (2026-11-06).** `rsk-analyzer::terms` is inside `verus!`: `derive_all` returns, for every Task, O_i as `spec_o` (the Table 13-1 sum of R29.3: four per-Job parameters, Δ_timer per timed event, Δ_lock_in + Δ_lock_out per Critical_Section site, Δ_hm, Δ_entry, the FP pair when the set has an FPU-using Task, Δ_irq + Δ_stamp for a Sporadic_Task), E_i as `spec_e` (R29.4 by the Partition's Overrun_Response, with L_i the longest site), B_i as `spec_b` (R29.2: the maximum of L_kernel, the stop and restart work of R14.10, and the candidates of every site of a lower Task on a Resource whose Ceiling reaches P_i, the cross-Partition clause included) with a site that attains it, and J_i = J_rel; `ticks_to_cycles_down` and `_up` are the rounding of R29.8; `derive_all` returns the overflow of R29.6 exactly when a term exceeds u64 (`prepare` then rejects instead of saturating, which the earlier code did). What stays outside the proof: the resolution of the Task_Model into the module's inputs (`inputs::prepare`: the Partition of each Task, the Resource of each site by name, the WCET_Record of each item, tested per rule), and the composition of `fixpoint` over the Tasks with the verdict of R29.5 (`response_times`, checked against the reference implementation). The three mutations of 9.2 each break one clause and are killed by the postcondition.
- **R29.9 on a scale.** `utilization_exceeds_one` is proven to compute Σ ⌈E_j · 10^9 / T_j⌉ > 10^9 in u128, and `lemma_scaled_term_over` that each scaled term is at or above the exact one, so a set with Σ E_j / T_j > 1 is always failed early; a set within 10^-9 per Task of full utilization can be failed early although the fixed point might have passed it. The step from the per-term inequality to the rational sum is proven since 2026-11-15: `utilization_over_one` states Σ E_j / T_j > 1 over the rationals as the inequality of the sum's numerator Σ E_j · Π_{k ≠ j} T_k and its denominator Π T_k (`util_num`, `util_den`), `lemma_scaled_sum_over` shows by induction that the scaled sum times the denominator is at or above 10^9 times the numerator, and `utilization_exceeds_one` ensures `utilization_over_one(loads) ==> r` (rsk-analyzer: 97 items verified, 0 errors). The converse does not hold and is not claimed (the pessimism above).
- **The intake terms of R19.2 (2026-11-06).** R19.2 asks for ⌈t / MIT⌉ · Δ_irq on every Task whose execution the handling can preempt, and the intake handler runs at the Kernel level, above every Task. The derivation had the cost in O_j of the Sporadic_Task only, so a Task above the source's Task (the demo's `control` above `echo` and `pulse`) carried none of it. `terms::terms_for` (proven to list `spec_terms`) now adds, for every lower-Priority Sporadic_Task j, the term ⌈w / MIT_j⌉ · Δ_irq with no jitter; for a Task of hep(i) the cost stays in E_j, whose ⌈(w + J_j) / T_j⌉ instances are at least ⌈w / T_j⌉. The report lists these terms as `(intake, R19.2)`, the reference implementation carries them, and the UPPAAL model records them as an approximation (R31.2). On the QEMU demo with placeholder timing `control` had been at exactly its deadline (B = `sensor`'s whole Budget), so the 300-cycle term failed it: `sensor`'s Budget in the QEMU declaration is 8.75 ms since (8.8 ms stays in the HIL configurations, whose binaries are unchanged); `control` has 650 cycles of slack.

### 5.17 The assembly entry and exit stubs (2026-11-13)

Until this date the stubs of `unsafe_module/stubs.rs` were justified by review (UJ-020 to UJ-028) and section 5.16 listed as assumed that a stub hands `epilogue_core` each plan it is given until the plan is 0. They are now proven against a machine model, `arch/cm4/stub_model.rs` (ghost code under `cfg(verus_keep_ghost)`; no build compiles it and PAR-01 does not count it).

**The model.** `M` is the processor state a stub touches: R0 to R12, the main stack pointer, LR, PSP, CONTROL, IPSR, PRIMASK, FAULTMASK, the Z flag, S16 to S31, and memory as a function of the address. Each instruction form the stubs use is one function on `M` (`i_*`, 32 forms; a conditional instruction of an `it` block is one function that tests Z). Each stub is the sequence of those functions, one per assembly line, split at its calls and branches (`irq_to_call`, `irq_after_call`, `irq_dispatch`, `irq_restore`, `irq_return`, `irq_to_unwind`; `unwind_entry`, `unwind_to_call`, `unwind_after_call`, `unwind_exit`, `unwind_again`; `svc_to_call`, `mm_to_call`, `fault_to_call`, `timer_to_call`, `kentry_after_call`, `kentry_return`). A call of a Rust half (`call`, `call_out4`, `call_ret`, `call_svc`) keeps what AAPCS leaves to the caller (R4 to R11, SP, S16 to S31, the memory at and above SP) and makes everything else arbitrary; its result enters as the verified core's contract, for which the cores now ensure named relations: `irq_rel`, `epi_rel`, `kentry_rel` (`entry.rs`).

**The tie to the assembly.** `cargo xtask check` (`checks::stub_model`, 5 unit tests) reads the `naked_asm!` block of every naked function and the model functions of its stub and requires that they are the same sequence, instruction by instruction and operand by operand, with the control lines (`bl`, `cbz`, `bne`, `b`, `bx`, labels) in place; it checks each `it` block against the conditions of the instructions under it; an assembly line of an unknown form and a naked function with no model are errors (`job_end_trampoline`, one `svc` instruction, is listed as not modelled). The assembly therefore cannot change without the check failing until the model, and with it the proof, is changed.

**The main stack.** `Frame` is what a dispatch stub leaves on the main stack for its level (R3 to R11 and LR; below them S16 to S31 when the preempted context had a floating-point context). `world(state, mem, base, frames, tpsp)` states that the register records of the chain's entries (5.16) lie on the main stack from `base` upward in dispatch order, that each record's shape and saved LR are those of the EXC_RETURN value its level record keeps, that every level but the first preempted a Job in Thread mode on the process stack with nothing between the two records, and that while a record exists the Thread-mode context is on the process stack.

**The theorems** (all in `stub_model.rs`, 13 lemmas and theorems; `cargo-verus` on rsk-kernel: 250 verified, 0 errors; no resource limit raised):

| Theorem | Statement |
|---|---|
| `theorem_irq_stub` | Entered with `world` and its Rust half answering by `irq_rel`: the stub calls with IPSR, PSP, CONTROL and its LR, under PRIMASK, with the out-pointer inside its own stack room. On a dispatch it installs the plan's PSP and CONTROL (`CONTROL_JOB`), returns with `EXC_RETURN_THREAD_PSP`, PRIMASK clear and FAULTMASK set, and `world` holds with the new level's register record on top and `arch_inv(.., 0)`. Without one it restores R3 to R11, LR, SP and S16 to S31; with no plan it returns as entered (PRIMASK clear, FAULTMASK set) with `world` and `arch_inv(.., 0)`; with a plan it reaches `rsk_unwind` with the plan in R0, PRIMASK held, the topmost record at the stack's top, and `arch_inv` with that plan. |
| `theorem_unwind` | From that state, for any number of rounds: each round calls `epilogue_core` with the plan the stub holds, which satisfies that core's `requires` (so the hypothesis of 5.16 is discharged, not assumed); pops exactly the register record of the level the core unwound (the pop's shape follows the EXC_RETURN value the core returns, which is the one the record was pushed under); goes round again exactly while the core names a next level. It leaves by an exception return with the EXC_RETURN value the last unwound level saved at its dispatch, the PSP and CONTROL of the context that level preempted, R3 to R11 and S16 to S31 as that dispatch pushed them, PRIMASK clear and FAULTMASK set. Then `pending_unwind == 0`, `arch_matches` holds, and `world` holds for the remaining levels. |
| `theorem_svc_stub`, `theorem_memmanage_stub`, `theorem_fault_stub`, `theorem_timer_stub` | The handler passes its arguments (the SVC: the caller's frame address by EXC_RETURN bit 2, and LR; the faults: `in_kernel` = PRIMASK set or taken from Handler mode, MemManage also the frame's address and LR), restores R4 and LR, and with no plan returns as entered with `world` and `arch_inv(.., 0)`, with a plan reaches `rsk_unwind` as above. |

Together with 5.16: from boot, every handler activation that returns leaves `arch_inv(.., 0)` and `world`, and an activation that ends Jobs returns into the context of the Kernel's executing Job with that context's registers. The entry-instruction window of 5.8a is covered by the same theorems: an activation entered from Handler mode plans neither a dispatch nor an unwind (`irq_rel`'s last clause, `fault_core`'s `in_kernel ==> r.0 == 0`), so its stub returns with every register, the stack pointer and `world` as it found them, which is the precondition of the preempted stub's own theorem.

**What the cores gained for this:** `irq_entry_core` ensures `irq_rel` (the records are untouched without a dispatch; a dispatch adds one chain entry whose record keeps the entry's EXC_RETURN and changes no other; an entry from Handler mode returns no frame and no plan); `epilogue_core` ensures `epi_rel` (the chain loses its last entry, no other record changes, and the PSP, CONTROL and EXC_RETURN returned are that record's); `svc_core`, `fault_core`, `timer_core` and `stack_violation_core` ensure `kentry_rel`; `fault_core` ensures `in_kernel ==> r.0 == 0`; `link_ok` bounds a record's exception number by 0x1FF, so `epilogue_core`'s mask leaves a plan as it is (`lemma_plan_number`).

**Findings of the proof.**

1. Fixed. A frame address of 0 from `plan_frame` would have been read by the stub as "no dispatch" (`cbz r0`) after `Kernel::dispatch` had started the Job. Nothing excluded it in the contracts (no stack is at address 0 on the Target). `irq_entry_core` now refuses a zero frame before the Kernel's dispatch, as it refuses an overflow.
2. Recorded, not changed. `rsk_timer_entry` (with `arch::timer` and `rsk_timer_rust`) is in no vector table: the nRF52840's time base is an interrupt line served by `rsk_irq_entry`. Unlike the fault stubs it has no Handler-mode guard and unlike `irq_entry_core` its shell does not turn a plan into an owed unwind, so bound to a vector it would, at a dispatch stub's entry instruction, unwind over the processor's own exception frame. `theorem_timer_stub` states this as a premise (a plan only for an entry from Thread mode). Either the stub is removed or it gets the guard before a Target binds it.
3. Recorded, not changed. A dispatched Job starts with R4 to R11 and S16 to S31 as the stub leaves them: R5 to R7, R9 to R11 and S16 to S31 hold the preempted Job's values, possibly another Partition's; R4 holds an address of the Kernel's main stack and R8 the EXC_RETURN value. No requirement of R15 to R17 forbids it (they bound memory access); it is an information flow between Partitions through registers, and clearing the registers on the dispatch path would close it at the cost of a few instructions per dispatch.

**Still assumed, by inspection** (the trust base of this proof): the meaning of each instruction function and the assembler; the exception model (`hw_entry`: an entry's LR describes the interrupted context and that context's frame is stacked on its own stack, HW-06; an exception return resumes the context its value names and unstacks its frame, and clears FAULTMASK, HW-07; handlers of one priority do not preempt each other, HW-01; an SVC is taken from Thread mode); that the compiled Rust halves follow AAPCS, write neither PRIMASK, FAULTMASK, PSP nor CONTROL, and write main-stack memory only in their own frames, through their out-pointer, and (the SVC handler) in the caller's exception frame; that the shells of `arch/cm4/mod.rs` and the `extern "C"` functions of `rsk-entry` call the verified core and return its plan unchanged (safe Rust outside `verus!`, generic over the system crate and instrumented by features; the SVC shell's stale-SVC and stack-check paths return 0 or `stack_violation_core`'s plan); that 32-bit arithmetic does not wrap on the main stack pointer; and, as in 5.16, the proof is of the build with the deferral of 5.8a.

Mutations, each killed by a proof failure: `stub-unwind-pops-before-fp`, `stub-irq-exc-return-not-kept`, `stub-irq-out-pointer-offset` (the model; each would also fail the tie check), `epilogue-returns-other-exc-return`, `fault-in-kernel-not-kernel`, `handler-mode-entry-plans-unwind`, `dispatch-frame-zero` (the cores).

### 5.18 What sections 4, 5.16 and 5.17 left to inspection, now contracts (2026-11-13)

Each item below was "by inspection and test" in this plan until this date. `cargo-verus` on rsk-kernel: 272 verified, 0 errors; rsk-logic: 341 verified, 0 errors. No resource limit was raised. Seven functions are checked in solver instances of their own (`#[verifier::spinoff_prover]`: `apply_core`, `boot_core`, `irq_entry_core`, `epilogue_core`, `svc_core`, `endpoint_reinit`, `dma_request_core`): in the solver context shared with the functions before them their proofs passed or ran out of the limit depending on unrelated changes elsewhere in the file; alone each passes inside the limit it had. `irq_entry_core`'s dispatch arm, intake arm and unbound arm, `svc_core`'s slower services and `boot_core`'s vector loop are verified functions of their own for the same reason (`dispatch_vector_core`, `dispatch_job_core`, `intake_core`, `unbound_core`, `svc_service_core`, `boot_vectors`; inlined).

| Fact | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| The executing Partition and the loaded MPU view (A6; audit finding 12) | The loaded view's value in no postcondition | `arch_inv` states that a level record's Partition is its Task's, that the Partition it names as preempted is the Partition of the chain entry below, and that `ArchState::current_partition` is the last dispatched Job's or none. `view_matches`: while a Job is dispatched and not unwound the loaded view is that Partition's; required and ensured by `irq_entry_core` and `epilogue_core`, kept by every other core. `lemma_executing_partition`: with nothing owed, `current_partition` and the loaded view are those of the Kernel's executing Job, and `current_partition` is none exactly when no Job is in progress (which the stack checks of R16.5 rely on). Mutations `unwind-skips-view-restore`, `dispatch-records-other-partition`. |
| Vector priorities at boot (A3; finding 12) | `HwModel.priority` in no contract | `boot_core` ensures `boot_vector_ok` for every interrupt line: a dispatch vector at `encode_level` of its Task's Priority, an intake or Kernel line at the Kernel level, each enabled, an unbound line disabled. Mutation `boot-vector-priority-kernel-level`. |
| The pended vector (A4; finding 12) | `HwModel.pending` in no contract | `apply_core` ensures (`vectors_applied`) that a vector it pends is the dispatch vector of a Task the Kernel found eligible, and that it pends nothing else. That the selected Task's vector *is* pended is not stated (`select` has no functional specification, only its properties). Mutation `apply-pends-other-vector`. |
| The intake enables (A13) | Not proven: `Tables` stated no distinctness of vectors | `apply_core` ensures that every intake vector is enabled exactly when its source is unmasked and its Partition not stopped. Mutation `apply-enables-masked-source`. |
| The vector tables (A12, A13) | A Generator fact | The tables have specifications (`spec_binding_of`, `spec_dispatch_vector_of`, `spec_intake_vector_of`, `spec_irq_count_of`); `vectors_ok` checks at boot that each Task's dispatch vector is bound to its dispatch and its intake vector to its intake (a new boot check, `BOOT_FAIL_VECTORS`, bit 16), which gives `tables_wf` to every core: no two Tasks share a vector and no intake vector is a dispatch vector, by proof. What remains of the Generator: that the table functions are pure functions of their arguments (constants of the generated crate). Mutation `boot-skips-vector-check`. |
| The Endpoint copy's confinement (R42.2; finding 13) | `endpoint_args` ensured nothing of the pointer; the copies had no `requires` | `endpoint_args` ensures the buffer is word-aligned, of the payload's length, and inside the RAM interval of the calling role's Partition (`message_permitted_spec`), and that the executing Job is the role's Task; `endpoint_slot` that a slot lies inside the Endpoint storage (`storage_permitted`); the cores copy through `copy_in_checked` and `copy_out_checked` only, which require both. Mutations `endpoint-args-skips-message-permitted`, `endpoint-slot-outside-storage`. |
| A-P6 for a Partition's stop or restart (finding 14) | A standing assumption | `clear_channels` and `endpoint_reinit` ensure `reinit_done` for every Endpoint: with a role of the Partition its channels are empty, its delivery latches clear and the roles of its other Partitions told; otherwise its channels are untouched. Mutation `stop-reinit-skips-peer-flag`. |
| Two mutation kills by the solver's resource limit (5.16, contested finding I) | `lock-by-other-than-top`, `dispatch-below-ceiling` | `lemma_no_new_blocking_section` and `lemma_no_lower_dispatch_while_waiting` take the step relation from `lemma_step_rel` and no longer reveal the history's quantifiers; both mutants now fail a postcondition and nothing else. The full register run of this date showed two more that the audit had not counted, `mit-run-spacing-dropped` and `overrun-not-marked-reported`; each now has a refuted obligation in its output (`lemma_mit_spacing_over_run`'s postcondition, taken from `releases_spaced` alone; an assertion that the Overrun is marked reported, in `budget_event`), and for each the solver also runs out of its resource limit on another function of the mutated file (`lemma_history_push`; `apply_response`), which is the first error the run prints. Every mutant of the register is therefore rejected by a refuted obligation; two also show a resource-limit failure. |
| The shells' decisions (5.16, 5.17) | Unverified code of `arch/cm4/mod.rs` and `rsk-entry` | Verified cores: `svc_of_ended_job_core` (an entry other than the SVC handler that ended a Job drops the pending SVCall; called last by the timer, intake, unbound, dispatch-vector and fault cores, which ensure it); `svc_check_core` (the SVC's stack check of R16.5: `None` only when the frame is on the main stack, no Job executes, or the frame lies in the executing Partition's stack region, with nothing changed; otherwise the stack violation's plan and the stacking fault discarded); the stale SVC is a branch of `svc_core`; `fault_frame_readable` guards the read of a faulting frame. A shell now fetches the Kernel, the hardware, the tables and the state, calls one core with the stub's arguments, records its instrumentation, enters the safe state when the core says so, and returns the core's plan: that remaining shape is by inspection. `KernelArch` gained `svc_clear_pending` and `stacking_fault_discard`, two trusted functions (proof_assumptions.toml: nine now) that were calls of unverified code before. Mutations `kernel-entry-keeps-pending-svc`, `svc-check-reads-any-frame`, `stack-violation-keeps-stacking-fault`, `fault-frame-read-after-stacking-failure`. |
| The entry-instruction window (5.8a, 5.17) | An argument in prose over the per-activation theorems | `stub_model.rs` states the exception model at a handler as functions (`exc_enter_handler`, HW-06: the frame stacked on the main stack, LR the Handler-mode EXC_RETURN; `exc_return_handler`, HW-07: R0 to R3, R12 and LR back from the frame, FAULTMASK cleared) and proves `theorem_nested_irq` and `theorem_nested_fault`: an interrupt vector or a fault taken at a handler's entry instruction, answered as the cores' contracts state for an entry from Handler mode, returns to that handler with every register, its stack pointers, S16 to S31 and the memory at and above its stack pointer as it left them, and with the main stack and the records in agreement for the new Kernel state: the precondition of the preempted stub's own theorem. The model functions themselves, and that handlers of one priority do not preempt each other, stay assumptions (5.17). |

Behaviour that changed: a boot with vector tables that disagree fails (bit 16); a dispatch refuses a frame address of 0 (5.17); `boot_core` clears the level records and `current_partition`. Nothing else: the functions split out of `irq_entry_core`, `svc_core` and `boot_core` are inlined.

Still by inspection after this section: the shells' remaining shape (above); the purity of the generated table functions; the model's instruction and exception semantics, AAPCS for the compiled Rust halves, and the assembler (5.17); the nine trusted functions of `Cm4Hw`; that `select`'s Task is the one whose vector is pended.

### 5.19 The Config_Checker's recomputation core (2026-11-13)

The Config_Checker (R26) recomputes the derived configuration values from the declared ones, independently of the Generator. Its arithmetic is now verified: `crates/rsk-confcheck/src/derive_v.rs`, inside `verus!`, verified by `cargo verus verify -p rsk-confcheck` in the verification job (19 items, 0 errors). The specifications are stated in the crate from the requirements; nothing of the Generator, the Kernel or their proofs is imported (R26.1: the crate depends on a JSON parser, the ELF reader, and since this date the Verus library).

| Value class (R26.2) | Function | Proven |
|---|---|---|
| Ceilings (R23.1) | `ceiling_of` | The maximum of the accessors' Priorities: at least each, equal to one of them, 0 for none |
| Priority encoding (DD-02) | `encode_level` | `(8 - p) * 32` for 1 to 8, 0 otherwise |
| Priority map | `levels_of` | The Priorities in use, each once, strictly ascending |
| Lock-stack capacity (R23.4) | `sum_checked` | The sum of the nesting depths; `None` exactly on overflow |
| Timer slots (R9.4) | `timer_slots`, `slot_index` | `3n + 2`; `3i + k`; `None` exactly on overflow |
| Regions (DD-07, R16) | `size_of`, `aligned`, `disjoint`, `stack_fits` | Two regions are reported disjoint exactly when no address lies in both; alignment and the stack's fit as defined; no overflow for any base and size |

Defects the proof removed (each a crash or a wrong answer of the checker on a malformed Task_Model, none reachable from a Task_Model the Generator emits): a Priority above 8 underflowed the encoding before its range was reported; a `size_log2` of 64 or more overflowed the shift; a base near the top of the address space overflowed a region's end. One check was added: no Partition's RAM lies over another Partition's code (R16.7); only code against code and RAM against RAM were compared.
