Over it, `lemma_dma_footprint_in_partition` (Verus on `rsk-logic` alone: 346 verified, 0 errors) shows that every byte of a DMA-permitted buffer of a Partition lies in that Partition's RAM above its stack and in no memory of the Kernel or of another Partition; and the Kani harness `dma_program_writes_the_pointer_and_the_count_and_nothing_else` shows that the function behind `Action::DmaProgram` writes exactly the pointer and the count register it is given. So the chain from the request to the bytes is: the postcondition of `dma_request_core` (what is programmed), the harness (the two register writes), HW-15 (what the peripheral then touches), the lemma (where those bytes are). HW-15 itself is checked on the board by the step "EasyDMA footprint (HW-15)" of the plain measurement run: the receive buffer of the traffic scenario lies between two 8-byte guards (`rsk::DmaGuarded`); at its second Job `sampler` takes the receive transfer back, which stops the traffic and waits for STOPPED, finds both guards as written and the buffer overwritten by the transfers of the 100 ms before, lends the buffer again and restarts the traffic. Passed (`docs/hil/measure_nrf52840_measure.md`). Its first two runs failed with every guard byte reading zero on both sides, which no overrun produces: the guards had been given their value as the static's initial value, and a Partition's statics are zeroed whatever value they declare. The guards are now written at run time.

**Findings of this change.** (1) A per-Task state or Resource declared with a non-zero initial value is zero at run time, at reset and after every restart, and the Generator accepts the declaration without a diagnostic. The design states that every Partition static is zero-initialized by construction; nothing checked it. Closed the same day (next paragraph). (2) The host doubles of the two EasyDMA supervisor calls wrote their record through the Kernel-side register double, which has asserted the Kernel's address precondition since 2026-11-06, so no host test could make a request; they now use the Partition-side double. (3) `sampler`'s measured maximum: 20,571 cycles in the plain measurement run (25,786 before, when every Job made a request) and 39,127 in the short stress run (37,815 before), within their Budgets with the margin.

Not shown at that point: the library tested and not proven, the peripheral's window address the Task's argument, HW-15 checked at one rate; each is taken up in the next paragraph. The hour-long configurations were not run on this build.

**The same day, later: the zeroed statics checked at compile time, and three gaps of R17 narrowed.**

- **A Partition static's initial value.** The Generator emits every cell (per-Task state, Resource, role state) through `ResourceCell::for_static`, a constant function that asserts every byte of the initial value is zero (`all_bytes_zero`, UJ-063, review). A declaration with a non-zero initial value no longer compiles, with the message naming R14.5; neither does a state type with padding bytes, whose value at those bytes is undefined (the compiler's own diagnostic). Compile-fail member `api_static_nonzero`. The check found one case in the tree at once: the isolation configuration declared `state filter.fpscr: u32 = 0xFFFF_FFFF` as a mark of "the probe has not written this", which was never in memory, so an unwritten record read 0, a value the rig's HW-10 row accepts when the declaration asks no flush-to-zero. The record now has a written flag, and the report omits the key when the probe did not run (the rig then lists the row as untested). On this build the row reads 0x1000000 against 0x1000000.
- **`dma_write_core` (R17.3).** The register-write service now ensures: a write is performed only for a channel of the executing Job's own Partition, at a word of that channel's 4 KiB window, to a register that is none of the pointer, count or list-mode registers of any channel of that window (`dma_reg_unmediated`); the model's record of such writes (`HwModel::dma_writes`) gains exactly that register and value; a refused request writes nothing; and the record of programmed transfers is untouched, as `dma_request_core` leaves the record of writes untouched. Verus on `rsk-kernel` alone: 274 verified, 0 errors. Two mutants registered, each run alone and killed (the mediated-register test dropped; the ownership test dropped).
- **The peripheral's address is not the Task's (R17.5).** The Generator emits each Partition's channels as constants (`rsk_target::DMA: [rsk::DmaChannel; n]`: the index in the system's DMA table and the peripheral's base address, from the table the Kernel's is generated from). `DmaBuf::lend` takes one of those; a Task supplies no index or address.
- **The lending rules by bounded model checking (R17.5).** Kani harness `bytes_are_out_of_reach_while_a_transfer_is_outstanding` (in `crates/rsk`, run alone: successful in 154 s): for every sequence of four operations on one buffer (read its bytes; lend it and keep or drop the transfer; take a kept transfer back; drop a kept transfer), with the peripheral's ENABLE register and STOPPED event set to any values before each, the bytes are handed out exactly when no accepted transfer is outstanding, a lend is accepted exactly when none is, and a transfer stops being outstanding only through a take-back that found the peripheral not enabled or reporting STOPPED. With the release rule broken on purpose (the buffer released without the event) the harness fails; the change was not kept. Bounds: four operations, a wait of two reads under Kani (1,024 on the Target), the host doubles of the supervisor calls (a request always accepted).
- **HW-15 at three rates.** The footprint step now runs at the three Jobs after the first, for the traffic of the 100 ms before each: at 8 Mbps, 1 Mbps and 125 kbps (the peripheral disabled while its rate is changed), the run going on at 8 Mbps. All three passed on the board: the buffer written, neither guard, the buffer read only after STOPPED. One peripheral (SPIM0); a second instance was not declared.

On the final source, each on its own: Creusot on the eight Partition 2 members (all proved), the Profile_Lint (no violation), the QEMU demo (RSK-OK), the Generator's tests, and on the rig the plain measurement, isolation and short stress configurations (all passed). `sampler`'s measured maximum: 22,255 cycles in the plain measurement run (the three footprint Jobs included), 39,135 in the short stress run. Not run: the full job, the hour-long configurations, the whole mutation register.

**Postconditions the requirements file exposed, and a defect one of them found (2026-11-16, later).** `docs/requirements_evidence.md` recorded three rules that were in function bodies and not in contracts. Each is now a postcondition (Verus on `rsk-kernel` alone: 274 verified, 0 errors; `rsk-logic` 346):

- **A send that is not accepted leaves every channel as it was and writes nothing into the Endpoint storage (R40.4, R40.6, R39.4).** Stating it failed to verify: `endpoint_send_core` took the slot (`channel_push`) and, when the slot's address could not then be formed (`endpoint_slot` returning nothing), returned "refused" with the slot counted and nothing copied into it, a message that was never sent and that the receiver would have taken. The path needs an inconsistent table to be reached, which the boot's validation excludes, but nothing in the function relied on that. The queue is now put back on that path.
- **A full buffer is reported (R40.5).** The log's newest event is the buffer-bound violation against the executing Job's Task and that Task's Partition (`KernelOps::spec_last_event`, from `Kernel::push_event`'s new clause that the pushed event is the log's last).
- **A failed boot has made nothing live (R12.2, R12.4).** `boot_core` returning false has enabled and pended no interrupt, armed no compare and not started the time base.

Three mutants registered, each run alone and killed (the restore removed; the report removed; a failing boot that starts the time base). On the changed Kernel: the response suite on the rig passed, with its Endpoint cases.

**HW-15 on a second instance (2026-11-16, later).** The plain measurement configuration's Partition 2 owns SPIM1 as well; at two further Jobs `sampler` stops SPIM0's traffic, runs SPIM1 on the same pins for 100 ms into a second guarded buffer, takes that transfer back and judges it, and restarts SPIM0. Passed on the board with the three rates of SPIM0 (`sampler`'s maximum 26,664 cycles, within its Budget with the margin). The unanswered stimulus pulse of the first hour-long run (2026-11-01) is as it was: no later run reproduced it, and the hour-long configurations were not run for this revision.

**`docs/requirements_evidence.md` covers the 61 requirements (2026-11-16, later).** Twenty-two rows are written from the text of the contracts and theorems they cite (63 statements, each hashed). Thirty-nine are written from the requirement's text, the plan and the matrix's facts, and say so where the tool's source was not read: their judgment is of the kind of evidence, not of the tool's code. 5 read "no" (R5, R11, R36, R56, R60: R5, R36, R56 and R60 are not implemented, Phase P5; R11 has no formal statement, tests only), 5 "yes" (R1, R2, R4, R8, R47: met as asked), the other 51 "weaker" with the reason.

### 11.8 Hardware_Model differential tests (2026-11-02, task 3.3, R49.3)

**The tests.** The rsk-kernel feature `hw-dt` (HIL builds only) adds two privileged boot phases and four Task-side probes, each recording into `DT_RECORD` (no-init RAM, left alone by the reboot the board takes on probe detach) or the per-Task state, and `rsk-hil::dt` compares every observation with the Hardware_Model's prediction, one row per observation and one verdict per rule (`docs/hil/hw_model_differential_nrf52840.md`, from the isolation suite's run). The early phase runs before `boot_core`, with the NVIC and the MPU at reset, and uses two vectors the Application leaves unbound (IRQ 30 and 31) and two SVC numbers the Kernel does not use (14, 15), which `irq_entry` and `svc` hand to the phase's own handlers while it is active: the implemented priority bits and the DD-02 encoding read back (HW-01); BASEPRI and BASEPRI_MAX (HW-03); both probe exceptions pended under PRIMASK and released by CPSIE I under BASEPRI 0x80, the more urgent taken and the other held until BASEPRI clears (HW-02, HW-03, HW-04); the arbitration order with unequal priorities and with equal ones pended in the opposite order, the lower exception number first (HW-05); IABR and ICSR after the returns (HW-07); Thread mode made unprivileged, a CPSID I there ignored, an SVC from it served privileged, the probe exception's handler reading CONTROL.nPRIV and the SCS and handing the privilege back (HW-04, HW-08); the SVC exception frame with patterns in R0 to R3 and R12, the stacked PC, xPSR.T, and the frame's alignment (HW-06); CYCCNT counting and wrapping (HW-13); and the cycles from one handler's exit to the next's entry with the next already pending against pended after the return (HW-14, tail-chaining). The late phase runs after `boot_core`: FPCCR, FPDSCR, ACTLR, CCR, MPU_CTRL, and PRIGROUP read back (HW-10, HW-11, HW-07, HW-09, HW-01) and the TIMER compare rule on CC[2], a channel the time base does not use, for distances of 0 to 16 ticks (HW-12). The Task-side probes are `filter`'s (an FPU Task): FPSCR as its first Job finds it and, every Job, R2 to R5, R8, R10, R12 and S0 to S31 loaded with patterns, a spin of about 3 ms during which control, echo, sensor, and the Kernel preempt it, and the registers compared (HW-06, HW-10, Property 2); and the isolation suite's matrix (HW-09, including the stack-overflow write) and its PPB attempts' BusFaults, precise with BFAR valid (HW-08, HW-11).

**Results (2026-11-02).** Thirteen rules match as stated: every observation of HW-01 to HW-11, HW-13, and HW-14's tail-chaining (154 cycles from exit to entry against 235 with a return between) is the model's. **HW-12 is a mismatch, and the model was wrong:** compares written 0 to 7 ticks ahead of the counter captured just before the write never fired, 8 ticks and more fired every time (the capture, the addition, and the write take about 7 ticks of the 16 MHz counter); the model's `MIN_COMPARE_DISTANCE` of 2 is refuted. Disposition: the constant is now 16 (twice the smallest distance that fired), the Kernel's `compare_target` (proven equal to `compare_for`) arms no compare closer, and the Kernel's correctness never rested on the constant alone: the nRF driver pends the time-base interrupt when the counter has passed the instant by the time the compare is written (11.1), which is what carried every run before the measurement. ASM-19 is confirmed as a real limit with its value.

**Not tested, with dispositions (R49.3):** the MSR PRIMASK and BASEPRI cases of HW-04 from unprivileged mode (no Task's code may contain them, PR-22, Link_Checker; the CPS case, the one `lemma_job_cannot_set_mask` rests on, is tested); the subregion-disable and overlap rules of HW-09 (no view of this Application sets SRD bits or has two enabled regions over one address; the encoding is Kani-checked at field level); late arrival in HW-14 (a window of a few cycles no probe can hit; observed in the field by the response suite's `SVC_STALE`, 11.4).

**Found while building the tests:** an SVC issued with PRIMASK set escalates to HardFault (the execution priority is already 0), so the unprivileged-mode probe holds its pending exception with BASEPRI, not PRIMASK; the early phase overwrote the run's record on the post-detach reboot until it learned to recognise that reboot by RESETREAS.DOG; the first CYCCNT wrap probe read the counter before it had wrapped. None is a Kernel finding.

### 11.9 Boot checks: mismatched values and corrupted tables (2026-11-04, task 7.2, R12.6)

**The suite.** The HIL build with the rsk-kernel feature `boot-mutation` reads a mutation word before its checks; the rig (`rsk-hil::boot_checks`) writes the word over the probe, flashes and runs the build, and judges the target's report, once per case, then runs the build with the word 0. The word lives at the top of the nRF52840's RAM, above every region of the layout: a no-init word of the Kernel's was overwritten by the flashing algorithm probe-rs loads at the start of RAM (the first run of the suite had every case boot clean). Each mutation makes exactly one check fail: a recorded value differing from the Target's (the CPUID, the implemented priority bits, the clock source, the recorded `CONFIG` and `LAYOUT` checksums, the interrupt-line count, through the trusted table accessors of `SysTables`), the `CONFIG` table corrupted in memory (a Task's Budget in the Kernel's copy, so the computed checksum differs), a register state the check rejects (PRIGROUP changed, the instruction cache enabled, written before the checks), and the watchdog started with a 50 ms reload and never fed (the reset lands inside the run; probe-rs is told not to catch the reset vector, so the next boot reports). A case passes when the report shows the safe state reached, the `boot_check` event with the failed check's bit and no other, every per-Task counter 0 (no Task released), and no event beyond the boot information (the watchdog case also logs the reset). The suite is the job's step `hil-boot-checks` (about two minutes) and `cargo xtask hil --boot-checks`.

**Results (2026-11-04, `docs/hil/boot_checks_nrf52840.md`):** pass, ten of ten cases, bits 8, 4, 10, 9, 11, 5, 5, 13, 6, and 7 each recorded alone, no Task released in any; the unmutated build passes afterwards. Without a case, by nature: the implementer and part-number checks and the MPU region count compare the Target against the Kernel's constants, not a recorded value, and the DWT and the FPU cannot be removed from the silicon; the host test `boot_checks_pass_on_the_expected_board_and_fail_one_bit_per_check` covers those on simulated registers.

**Found while building the suite:** probe-rs's flashing algorithm occupies the start of RAM (a mutation word there is lost); probe-rs halts at the reset vector after a watchdog reset unless told otherwise (`--no-catch-reset`); the demo's 200 ms run ends, and its safe state feeds the watchdog, before the 0.5 s reload elapses, so the watchdog case shortens the reload. None is a Kernel finding. The review of task 3.4 (11.10) changed the reset-reason read the watchdog case relies on (anomaly 136).

### 11.10 Errata review (2026-11-04, task 3.4, R11.6, R49.4)

**Sources.** The Arm Cortex-M4 and Cortex-M4 with FPU Software Developers Errata Notice v3 (ARM-ECM-0426805, 2019: six errata, all present in r0p0 and r0p1, the Target's r0p1 among them) and the nRF52840 Revision 3 errata (build code Fx0; the rig's boards are AAF0), 57 anomalies. The review table is the `hw_model.rs` header (R49.4): for each erratum and for each anomaly that touches a peripheral or behaviour the Kernel uses, its applicability, the workaround applied, and the test that shows the workaround in place.

**Errata of the core.** 838869 (a store buffered across an exception return can vector the next interrupt wrongly) applies: every naked stub ends with `dsb` before its exception return (DD-01), and the Link_Checker now checks it statically on every binary (`ERR-838869`: every `bx` of a naked stub immediately preceded by `dsb`, a `cpsie` between being no store), the test the task asked for; the hour-long runs of 7.3 and 7.5 ran with it. 776924 (VDIV/VSQRT with very short ISRs) requires lazy FP stacking, which the policy disables (FPCCR.LSPEN = 0, the notice's first workaround; `hil-dt-hw-10` reads it back). 752770 (interrupted loads to SP) has no instruction of its class in the Kernel; the Link_Checker now decodes every load to SP as a dynamic SP write, which fails the build for Kernel code (`ERR-752770` note). 839676 (fused MAC on subnormals with FZ clear) is outside the Kernel (no FP arithmetic, R6.6); the declaration's `fp_flush_to_zero = true` (2026-11-04) sets FPDSCR.FZ at boot so that every Job's FP context starts in flush-to-zero mode, the notice's workaround, and `hil-dt-hw-10` reads FPDSCR back as declared and sees `filter`'s first context start from it (the isolation configuration declares it; FPSCR 0x0100_0000 observed). 806422 (ITM with global timestamping) and 1299509 (FPB corruption on an asynchronous reset) concern debug blocks the Kernel never uses.

**Anomalies of the chip.** 136 (RESETREAS bits set beside RESETPIN after a pin reset) applied: the boot read DOG unconditionally and would have entered the safe state after a pin reset; `hw::boot_info` now ignores the other bits when RESETPIN is set and clears every set bit (the host test and the boot-check suite's watchdog case cover it). 155 (a GPIOTE IN event twice on close edges) is masked by the MIT policy and never met on the rig (203,333 of 203,333 edges answered once); 87 (a spurious wake from idle with the FPU) only re-enters the idle loop; 36 (CLOCK registers not reset) is why the boot starts and checks HFCLK explicitly; 78, 213, 242 do not apply (the time base never stops, no System OFF, no flash writes); 176 and 184 are bench procedures probe-rs follows; the SPIM3 anomalies do not reach the demo's SPIM0.

### 11.11 Cycle attribution: the Budget accounting against the DWT (2026-11-04, task 7.5, R18.1, ASM-06)

**The test.** The demo's cycle configuration (`nrf52840_cycles`, 2 seconds; feature `cycles` of part0 and part1) has `control` (Priority 7, the highest: nothing but the Kernel's own entries interrupts it) and `log` (Priority 2, released 4 ms into its 40 ms period so that `sensor`'s 6 ms Critical_Section, `control`, and the Kernel's work for them preempt every one of its Jobs) execute the same 8,000-iteration spin (`rsk::spin`) and nothing else per Job. The Kernel's record of R34.7 now keeps, per Task, the smallest execution and the sum of all beside the largest, and the rig (`rsk-hil::cycles`) judges: both Tasks ran enough Jobs; their mean executions agree within a tolerance; each Task's executions lie in a narrow band; `log`'s largest response time exceeds its largest execution several times over (it was preempted). The suite is the job's step `hil-cycles` and `cargo xtask hil --cycles`.

**Results (2026-11-04, `docs/hil/cycles_nrf52840_cycles.md`):** pass. `control` 200 Jobs, executions 74,637 to 80,991 cycles, mean 76,697 (1.198 ms); `log` 49 Jobs, 80,137 to 87,031, mean 82,195 (1.284 ms); `log`'s largest response 147,390 ticks (9.2 ms, 589,560 cycles) against its largest execution of 87,031 cycles. A Kernel that charged the preempting Jobs' cycles to the preempted one would have shown `log`'s executions some seven times `control`'s.

**The Kernel's entries by kind (task 19.3).** The same record now keeps every Kernel entry by kind (dispatch, timer, intake, each SVC, fault, epilogue, and the entries that restarted or stopped a Partition or applied an Overrun_Response: count, largest, sum of the Rust half's cycles) and, per Task, the entries that interrupted its Jobs. From it the rig emits, with the 25 % margin, the Kernel_Timing_Parameters Δ_dispatch, Δ_timer, Δ_detect, Δ_lock_in, Δ_lock_out, Δ_complete (the Job-end SVC plus the unwind's epilogue), Δ_svc, Δ_dma_write, Δ_hm, Δ_restart, Δ_stop, and Δ_enforce, whichever the run exercised, into every measurement configuration's record (11.5), and the cycle test emits Δ_entry (R13.6, design.md): the preempted Task's excess spread over the entries that interrupted its Jobs. The Analyzer charges Δ_entry once per Job instance, so that every interfering instance carries it (R29.4). The records of the cycle configuration join the measurement configurations' in the job's manifest and measured analyses.

**The floating-point variant (2026-11-06, task 19.3; Δ_fp_save and Δ_fp_restore).** `nrf52840_cycles_fpu` (`cargo xtask hil --cycles --fpu`; the job's `hil-cycles-fpu`) puts `filter`, the FPU Task, in `log`'s place as the preempted spinner (period 40 ms, offset 4 ms, Budget 6 ms): its low-pass step at the start of every Job makes the context active, so every Kernel entry that interrupts it stacks and unstacks the extended frame (DD-04, eager), which the Budget accounting charges to the Job outside the charge points as it charges the stub's cycles. Measured: `filter`'s excess over `control` 1,900 cycles per interrupting entry (136 entries over 49 Jobs; the means 77,150 and 82,424 cycles, 6.84 % apart against the variant's 15 % tolerance) against Δ_entry 1,782 of the plain configuration's run of the same day: 118 cycles of stacking per entry on the first run, and 1,791 against 1,800 on the campaign's run that evening, below the method's resolution (the difference of two per-entry means, whose run-to-run spread is about 100 cycles). The record therefore carries, per parameter, half of the measured difference or of the 100-cycle resolution, whichever is larger (50 cycles observed, 63 with the margin, when the difference is below it; the architecture's extended frame is 17 words each way, well below), with the condition saying which (`docs/hil/kernel_timing_nrf52840_cycles_fpu.json`, which joins the cycle configuration's record in every measured analysis). The last placeholders of Table 13-1 that an entry does not contain are bounded with this; Δ_release and Δ_wake stay inside their entries and Δ_stamp is measured (11.5).

**Measured: the attribution overhead.** `log`'s Jobs are charged 5,498 cycles (7.2 %, 86 µs) more than `control`'s (4,878 on the run before): at every Kernel entry that interrupts a Job, the cycles between the exception entry and the handler's charge point and between the last charge point and the exception return (the stub's pushes and pops, and in a HIL build the trace ring and the trace pins) are the interrupted Job's, about a dozen entries per `log` Job here. Corrected 2026-11-13 (the audit's contested finding F): the charge is larger than "some hundreds of cycles" and is per interrupting Kernel entry, not per preemption: `timer_core` charges at its entry only and the intake path has no charge point, so the whole Rust half of every Kernel entry that interrupts a Job is the Job's (Δ_entry, design Table 13-1). R18.1 is therefore met "within Δ_entry per interrupting Kernel entry"; the mean and maximum of Δ_entry are in the records of 11.5 and are restated from the campaign on ironoxide-eval-v3. The tolerance of the test is 10 % and the excess is reported in every run. For the Analyzer this is a per-preemption cost that lands on the preempted Job's Budget rather than on the preempting work: Table 13-1's switching parameters (Δ_sw, task 19.2) must cover it, and a Budget must leave room for it times the Job's possible preemptions (R29.4; the WCET_Records of 11.6 measured with the HIL instrumentation already include it, R34.7). A first version of the test with `control`'s lock and raise SVCs left in showed `control` 9.7 % above `log`: the SVCs' cost is the calling Job's own, charged as R18.1 requires.

### 11.12 The instrumentation's share of the measurements (2026-11-05, task 19.2, R34.7)

**Method.** The HIL build carries the trace ring (an entry pushed at every Kernel entry point), the trace pins (two register writes per transition), and the records themselves (the release-timing, execution, Critical_Section, and entry tables, written inside the handlers). A lean HIL build (`cargo xtask hil --cycles --lean`, `--stress --short --lean`: the rsk-kernel feature `record-only` beside `trace-ram`, no `trace-pins`) keeps the records and the event mirror, which the rig needs to judge the run, and drops the ring and the pins; its reports and records carry the suffix `_lean` (`docs/hil/*_lean.*`) and the job never takes them (R33.4 keys the records on the manifest's binaries). The share of the instrumentation is the difference between the two builds' figures for the same configuration on the same day; the records' own cost (the DWT reads and the table updates inside the handlers) stays in both and is bounded by the calibration of R34.3 (1 cycle per read) and a few dozen stores per entry.

**Results (2026-11-05; `docs/hil/kernel_timing_nrf52840_stress_short[_lean].json`, `..._cycles[_lean].json`, `wcet_nrf52840_stress_short[_lean].json`; the largest observed values before the margin, cycles):**

| Figure | Instrumented | Lean | Instrumentation's share |
|---|---|---|---|
| Δ_dispatch | 2,821 | 2,451 | 370 (13 %) |
| Δ_timer (and Δ_detect, Δ_restart, Δ_enforce: the same longest timer entries) | 4,498 | 3,765 | 733 (16 %) |
| Δ_lock_in | 452 | 291 | 161 (36 %) |
| Δ_lock_out | 1,744 | 1,604 | 140 (8 %) |
| Δ_complete | 3,202 | 2,847 | 355 (11 %) |
| Δ_svc | 232 | 158 | 74 (32 %) |
| Δ_dma_write | 698 | 545 | 153 (22 %) |
| Δ_view | 327 | 327 | 0 (0 %) |
| Δ_irq | 2,088 | 1,942 | 146 (7 %) |
| J_rel | 8,631 | 8,447 | 184 (2 %) |
| L_kernel | 4,498 | 3,765 | 733 (16 %) |
| Δ_entry (the cycle configuration) | 1,765 | 1,680 | 85 (5 %) |
| WCET of `part0.control` | 22,816 | 20,471 | 2,345 (10.3 %) |
| WCET of `part0.echo` | 9,420 | 8,591 | 829 (8.8 %) |
| WCET of `part0.sensor` | 407,738 | 404,920 | 2,818 (0.7 %) |
| WCET of `part1.log` | 204,239 | 202,854 | 1,385 (0.7 %) |
| WCET of `part1.pulse` | 12,251 | 12,119 | 132 (1.1 %) |
| WCET of `part2.filter` | 37,681 | 31,923 | 5,758 (15.3 %) |
| WCET of `part2.sampler` | 14,834 | 16,114 | -1,280 (-8.6 %) |

**Reading.** The ring and the pins cost each Kernel entry some 150 to 730 cycles of the Rust half (a ring push is a time-base read and three stores, a pin transition two register writes, both inside the handler), which is 8 to 16 % of the longer entries and about a third of the shortest (Δ_lock_in, Δ_svc), and about 5 % of Δ_entry (the pins' second transition and the ring's push at the stub's end lie outside the charge points). The Task entries' WCETs fall by 1 to 15 %: `filter` and `control`, the Tasks with the most Kernel entries per Job (locks, raises, the FPU save), by the most; `sensor`'s 6 ms section hardly at all. The short Critical_Sections (`control#cs:*`, `sampler#cs:stats`, under 10,000 cycles) differ between the two runs in both directions by up to 3,000 cycles: that is run-to-run variation of sections a few hundred cycles long whose measure includes any Kernel entry inside them (Δ_entry each), not the instrumentation, and the comparison is reliable for the Task entries and the Kernel_Timing_Parameters. The lean run's "Kernel trace" step read the 512 entries the previous instrumented run had left in the no-init ring (the lean build never writes it): the step's verdict is vacuous there. Disposition: the committed records stay those of the instrumented build (the job's binaries), conservative by these shares for a Flight_Build; a Flight_Build measured directly needs the records' own tables without the event mirror, which is task 19.2's remaining instrumentation work.

**The Flight_Build's features on the rig, measured from outside (2026-11-16; `cargo xtask hil --flight`, record `docs/hil/flight_loopback_nrf52840.md`).** The self-test of 11.2 over the Application built with `nrf52840,debug-port,quiet` (`FEATURES_FLIGHT`): the Flight_Build's features and the two the rig cannot do without. `debug-port` is one register write at boot; without it the access port locks at the first reset and the board must be erased to be flashed again. `quiet` silences the Tasks' debug prints, which need a host. No trace ring, no trace pins and no record are compiled (63,152 loadable bytes), so the Kernel reports nothing and the only measurement is the analyzer's, at 2 MHz, of the stimulus pulse against `pulse`'s answer on P1.10. Three runs of the demo's declared operating duration (200 ms), each passed:

| Build | Run | Pulses answered | Latency min (µs) | Median (µs) | Max (µs) | Later than 15 ms |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Flight features | 1 | 10 | 70.5 | 71.5 | 5,012.5 | 0 |
| Flight features | 2 | 10 | 70.0 | 71.5 | 4,445.5 | 0 |
| Flight features | 3 | 11 | 70.0 | 71.0 | 4,707.0 | 0 |
| HIL build (ring, pins, record) | 1 | 10 | 101.5 | 103.0 | 4,976.0 | 0 |

The path from the stimulus edge to `pulse`'s first instruction that toggles the pin (the intake entry, the dispatch, the Job's start) takes about 71 µs without the instrumentation and about 103 µs with it: some 32 µs, 2,000 cycles, are the ring's, the pins' and the record's on that one path, measured without any of the three in the binary. The maximum is the same in both builds (4.4 to 5.0 ms): it is `sensor`'s Critical_Section, which `pulse` waits out, and not Kernel time. What this is and is not: it is the first figure taken from a binary with none of the Kernel's instrumentation, and it confirms the direction of 11.12 (the instrumented records are the larger); it is one Task's response over 31 pulses in three runs of 200 ms, not a WCET_Record: no per-Task execution time, no Kernel_Timing_Parameter and no hour of PAR-05 can be read from a build that records nothing. Getting those needs either a record the Flight_Build keeps (which makes it a different build) or a trace taken by the probe from outside (ETM or ITM, which the DK's on-board probe does not carry). The item of section 13 stays open, reduced by this.

### 11.13 Loop-bound driving, the EasyDMA traffic scenario, and the branch coverage of the measurement inputs (2026-11-06, task 19.2; R17.4, R34.4, R34.5, R34.8, R34.9, R35.3, R35.4, R53.3)

**The measurement instrumentation.** The timing and stress configurations carry the Partition crates' `measure` feature (the system crate's `timing`, `timing-short`, `stress`, and `stress-short` features enable it): every bounded loop of the Application keeps its largest iteration count in per-Task state, part2's `sampler` runs the EasyDMA traffic scenario, and the safe state reports both. The bodies are proven under the feature as under every other (section 7: the members `part0-stress` and `part2-timing` joined the proof workspace for the pairs the feature creates; `traffic_step` states that it leaves the Job's count and the Resource as they were). The instrumentation's cost is in the records as the rest of the HIL build's (R34.7, 11.12).

**Loop-bound driving (R34.9, R35.3, R35.4).** The Profile_Lint's export `rsk-loop-bounds/1` is the machine-readable file of R35.3; `cargo xtask hil --release-timing|--stress` writes it for the Application before the run and judges the build's loop report against it: each loop is keyed `loop:<partition>:<function>:<label>` (every loop of the demo now carries a label, which names it in the export and the report), the safe state prints the largest iteration count a run reached, and the rig fails a loop above its bound (R35.4), passes one that reached it, and passes one below it only when `hil-rig.toml` (`[loops] unreachable`) records the bound as unreachable with a justification (R34.9); a loop the configuration never completed (an injected Job the Overrun_Response ends before the count is kept, or a loop under a feature the configuration lacks) is listed as not run. The report is `docs/hil/loop_iterations_<variant>.json` (`rsk-loop-iterations/1`) and a section of the run's report. Every loop of the demo is a wait by the time base with an iteration guard as the PR-16 backstop, one to four orders of magnitude above the iterations the wait takes (each iteration reads the time base through an SVC), so every bound is recorded unreachable with that justification. Observed (2026-11-06; timing-short, then stress-short): `sensor`'s Critical_Section hold 1,364 and 699 iterations against 10,000,000 (12 and 6 ms), `sensor`'s injected late Job 3,718 against 50,000,000 (38 ms; timing only), `log`'s 3 ms wait 342 and 352 against 10,000,000; `spin_ms` of the three crates and `filter`'s hold never completed (the Jobs that run them are ended by the Overrun_Response or the configuration lacks the feature). No loop exceeded its bound.

**The EasyDMA traffic scenario (R17.4, R34.5).** part2 owns the Application's only EasyDMA-capable peripheral, SPIM0, so the worst-case concurrent EasyDMA traffic of all Partitions is SPIM0 at its highest rate throughout: `sampler`'s first Job of each incarnation selects the pins (P0.14 and P0.15, the DK's LED2 and LED3, for SCK and MOSI; P0.16, LED4, for MISO; part1, the owner of `P0`, sets their directions in its first `log` Job, since the SPIM does not and does not clock without SCK connected), sets 8 Mbps and the END_START shortcut, enables the peripheral, requests a 64-byte transfer out of `tx` and one into `rx` (`rsk::DmaBuf::lend`, the buffers checked, R17.1), and starts it; the shortcut chains the transfers for the whole run, two AHB masters on the RAM at a byte per microsecond each way. Every later Job counts whether a transfer ended since the last (`dma_end`) and clears the event. The rig's step "EasyDMA traffic (R17.4)" judges the counters and SPIM0's registers at the end, and every record of the run names the scenario with what was observed, beside the floating-point scenario (triggered by `filter`'s Jobs, not by the others') and the preemption scenario (every Task at its rate, the stormed source under stress), as R33.1 and R34.5 require. Observed: timing-short, a transfer ended before 1,799 of 1,800 `sampler` Jobs, END set at the end; stress-short, the restarts of part2 every 200 ms re-initialize the counters and the traffic restarts with each incarnation's first Job (END and STOPPED set at the end, SHORTS cleared by the Kernel's stop).

**What the scenario needed and found.** (1) No Partition could start an EasyDMA transfer: its view maps the peripheral read-only (DD-08) and the only service programmed a transfer's pointer and count. The Kernel gained the register-write service DD-08 anticipated, `rsk::dma_write(channel, offset, value)` (SVC 6 with bit 31 of r0 set; `dma_write_core`, Verus-proven with the request's frame conditions; `Action::DmaWrite` in the Hardware_Model and the Kani harness), which writes any register of the Partition's own EasyDMA peripheral except the pointer, count, and list-mode registers of every channel of the window, and refuses the rest as a spatial violation (R17.2); Δ_dma_write covers both services. (2) **A Partition's END_START shortcut survived the Kernel's stop task (R17.6).** The first coverage run of the stress configuration found one restart of about ninety after which a transfer started again within 150 µs of the Kernel's stop task (the first Job of the new incarnation clears STARTED, waits, and reads it set again: `dma_after_stop`): the stop task ends the transfer with an END event, and the shortcut the Partition had set starts the next. The Kernel's stop sequence now clears the peripheral's SHORTS register before its stop tasks (`DmaCfg::shorts` from the Target table's per-class offset, `Action::DmaShortsOff`, Kani checked; Generated_Config regenerated), and the stress runs since then show SHORTS 0 and STOPPED set at the end with no transfer after a stop. The isolation suite (11.7) had not seen this because its Partition never set a shortcut. (3) The demo's safe state counted one accepted request per run under the scenario (`dma_ok`), its criterion `dma_ok + 2 >= sampler Jobs` failing the run; `traffic_step` now counts a confirmed transfer as the Job's accepted request.

**Observations of the first runs with the scenario (open).** (The UsageFault of 2026-11-05 recurred in the evening campaign of 2026-11-06 with its diagnostics, section 13.) The release-timing step's wire latency reached 15,160 µs once in 10,000 against `pulse`'s 15 ms deadline in one timing-short run (12,420 to 12,560 µs in the others), with no deadline miss logged by the Kernel: the Kernel's deadline runs from its timestamp of the event (R9.6), the wire's from the edge, and the difference is the stamp lag (an entry's masking, L_kernel, then the intake handler's timestamp, within Δ_irq; the dispositions of 11.5). The loopback step now lists what executed inside every late window from the trace pins, exports L_kernel (`l_kernel_cycles`, 7,000), and allows an answer later than the deadline by at most the stamp lag when the Kernel logged no miss (L_kernel plus Δ_irq until Δ_stamp was measured the same day, 11.5: 48.6 and 76.7 µs observed, 8,000 cycles exported, which the 160 µs of that run exceeds: a failure by that rule, not reproduced in the runs since). In the same run the agent's largest `control` Job exceeded the trace pin's longest run by 1,418 cycles (7.9 %; 204 cycles, 1.1 %, on the next run; 79 before the scenario) with 18,322 runs for 18,000 Jobs on the pins: the check's tolerance is now a tenth beside the two samples of quantization, and the run-count excess (a Job's run split by a Kernel entry that drives the pins, or a short run between Jobs) is to be read from the capture. Both stay listed in section 13.

**The 160 µs late answer, an attempt at the explanation (2026-11-07).** What the figure cannot be: a pairing artefact of the analyzer (each response edge answers the latest rise at or before it, so a dropped rise lengthens a latency by the 18 ms period and an extra rise shortens one; that run had 10,000 response edges for 9,999 answers, so no spurious edge either); a Kernel-side miss missed (KD-R9.6 serves an overdue deadline at the ending Job's observed instant, so a Job that answers before its end and ends before the deadline by the Kernel's clock is never late by more than the stamp lag plus the capture's two samples of quantization, 20 µs). So the answer was within 20 µs of the deadline by the Kernel's clock (its own latency about 15.0 ms, far above the 12.4 to 12.9 ms the other maxima show) and the stamp lagged the edge by about 160 µs. The single-entry basis of L_kernel does not reach that: the longest Rust half of one entry kind is 5,469 cycles (85 µs, the timer's) and Δ_irq 2,088 (33 µs), 118 µs with the capture's quantization 138; yet the hour-long run of 2026-11-07 already observed a stamp lag of 102 µs. The candidate is a masked span longer than any one entry: a timer entry that serves a deadline miss or Overrun of `sensor` (the configuration injects 8 late Jobs and records 16 misses and 8 Overruns a run) ends the Job and unwinds its level inside the same PRIMASK span, so the span is the timer's Rust half plus the epilogue's, 5,469 + 4,259 = 9,728 cycles (152 µs), which with Δ_irq gives 185 µs, above the 160 observed; a pulse edge lands in one of those 16 to 24 spans about once in eight 3-minute runs, and the coincidence with an answer at the deadline makes the observation the rarity it was. The consequence, if so, is not the one late wire answer but L_kernel itself: the record's longest single Rust half understates the longest masked span whenever an entry's response unwinds a Job, and the Analyzer's blocking term takes L_kernel. The test: the HIL build measures every masked span end to end (DWT at the stub's `cpsid i` and `cpsie i`), records its maximum, and the record carries L_kernel from that span rather than from the longest Rust half; the timing configuration should then show about 150 µs and the next late answer, if any, its own span from the loopback step's listing. Listed in section 13 against L_kernel's basis until measured.

**Measured the same day: the span is longer than the sum.** The HIL build now records the masked span end to end (`ARCH_SPAN`): a span opens at the first Rust half of a Kernel entry and closes at the Rust half whose return reaches a context in which a Kernel-level interrupt is taken, which the close decides from ICSR.VECTPENDING (a pending Kernel-level exception, or a dispatch vector the resumed BASEPRI admits, tail-chains into the next handler under PRIMASK and keeps the span open; an unwind keeps it open through its epilogues; a pending vector BASEPRI masks, or nothing pending, closes it, since the Job then resumes and an intake is taken). The rig's step "masked span (L_kernel)" reports the maximum, and the record's L_kernel is the maximum plus Δ_entry when the span record exists (the longest single Rust half otherwise). The first timing-short run on it: **18,748 cycles (293 µs) over 11.7 million spans, closed by a timer entry**, against the longest single Rust half of 5,469: a timer entry serving a release instant of the injected late Jobs, whose response ends the Job, unwinds its level, and tail-chains into the dispatch of the next one, all under one mask. The 160 µs answer lies inside it and is explained: the one-off was a `pulse` edge inside such a span with the Job's own answer at its deadline. The campaign of the same day measured every configuration: 205 to 310 µs in the short runs, 340.8 µs in the hour-long timing run (238 million spans), 408.2 µs in the hour-long stress run (144 million spans, closed by a dispatch entry: the storm's intakes chain into the dispatches they release), and 1.8 ms in the Kernel coverage build, whose instrumented Kernel is no timing evidence (section 14). The export `l_kernel_cycles` is 35,000 (the stress run's span plus Δ_entry with the margin), and the Analyzer's blocking term takes each record's own value from this refresh on. **The distribution (the same day, the record's histogram by binary order of magnitude and its count and maximum per closing entry; the reports' section "The Kernel's masked spans").** The timing-short run: 11.3 million spans, 11.07 million of them the 4 to 8 µs of a `now` SVC, 15,251 between 128 and 256 µs, one above (the 260.8 µs maximum, closed by a dispatch); by closing entry, the dispatches' longest 260.8 µs, the timer's 230.9, the epilogues' 61.9, the Job ends' 37.3. The stress-short run: 129 spans above 256 µs (the longest 413.3 µs, closed by a dispatch; the timer's 302.0, the epilogues' 195.3). So a span above 128 µs is not rare but about 85 a second under the timing configuration, and the longest ones are the chains that end in a dispatch: the time base's entry, the response or release it serves, and the dispatch it pends. That is the Kernel's non-preemptible section as built, and L_kernel now states it; the records of the hour-long and coverage runs predate the histogram (their binaries differ by the record alone) and the job notes them stale and unused (R33.4) until the next campaign, the short records being fresh.

**Branch coverage of the measurement inputs (R34.4, R53.3).** `cargo xtask hil --coverage [--stress]` builds the short configuration as the coverage build: the three Partition crates compiled with `-C instrument-coverage -Z coverage-options=branch -Z no-profiler-runtime` by the coverage toolchain (`toolchain/decision.toml [coverage]`: nightly-2026-09-17, rustc 1.100.0-nightly, LLVM 23.1.1, since the pinned 1.95.0 has neither the branch option nor a way to omit the profiler runtime; every crate keeps the legacy symbol mangling the Link_Checker reads), through a `RUSTC_WRAPPER` the command writes; the Kernel, the API, and the declaration as in every HIL build. The counters lie in `.p<i>.cov` of the generated linker script, in each Partition's RAM above its image and outside its range of the zero table (a restart re-initializes the image and keeps the counts), zeroed at instant 0 by the Kernel's `coverage` feature (`__rsk_cov_table`; the reboot the probe's detach causes never reaches instant 0, so the counts survive it as the records do); rustc lowers every counter update to an atomic 64-bit add the Cortex-M4 has no instruction for, so the Kernel provides the libcall `__atomic_fetch_add_8` in the shared code region (not atomic: a lost increment changes a count, never whether a branch executed). The profile data and names stay in flash under their own section names (llvm-cov looks `__llvm_prf_names` up by name), the coverage mapping outside the image. The run is the measurement run (the same stimulus, loop report, and scenario steps; it must pass, and it writes no timing record: the instrumented bodies are not the measured binary). Afterwards the rig reads the counters over the probe, assembles an LLVM raw profile (format version 11, the 32-bit magic, the data records copied from the ELF with each counter pointer rewritten to the joined block; checked against `llvm-profdata` by `profraw_round_trips_through_llvm_profdata`), and runs `llvm-profdata merge` and `llvm-cov export` over the Partition sources. Each Task entry's figure is over the branch outcomes (true and false of every branch) of its body, its closures, and its crate's helpers (every function of the crate that is no Task's body or closure: the Partition bodies are inlined into their wrappers, so the binary's call graph has no call to attribute a helper by, and a helper counts for every Task of its crate, which can only lower a figure); each Critical_Section site takes its Task's figure. The threshold is **50 %** for Phase 3 (`hil-rig.toml [coverage] branch_percent`): the uncovered outcomes are listed in every run's report (`docs/hil/coverage_<variant>.md`, `.json`, schema `rsk-branch-coverage/1`) and are, in both configurations, the fault paths and backstops the configuration cannot drive (below); a run with an item below the threshold fails (R34.4). The figures enter the committed WCET_Records of the configuration as `coverage_branch_percent` with a condition naming the coverage build (R33.1), and the measured analyses of the job carry them.

**Results (2026-11-06; `docs/hil/coverage_nrf52840_timing_short.json`, `..._stress_short.json` (the stress figures of the day's last run, after the Budget revision of 11.14), `..._measure.json`; branch outcomes covered of outcomes counted):**

| Item | Timing-short | Stress-short | Plain (11.14) | Uncovered outcomes and why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `part0.control` | 100 % (4 of 4) | 100 % (4 of 4) | 100 % (4 of 4) | none |
| `part0.echo` | 100 % (none) | 100 % (none) | 100 % (none) | the body has no branch |
| `part0.sensor` | 85 % (12 of 14) | 83 % (5 of 6) | 83 % (5 of 6) | the iteration guards of the Critical_Section hold and of the injected late Job (`i < 10_000_000`, `i < 50_000_000`: the PR-16 backstops the time guard precedes, the unreachable bounds of the loop report) |
| `part1.log` | 92 % (13 of 14) | 90 % (20 of 22) | 92 % (13 of 14) | the 3 ms wait's guard (the same backstop); under stress the loop-report closure of the injected spin, whose Job the Overrun_Response ends before it |
| `part1.pulse` | 100 % (6 of 6) | 100 % (10 of 10) | 100 % (6 of 6) | none |
| `part2.filter` | 50 % (6 of 12) | 70 % (17 of 24) | 50 % (6 of 12) | the crate's helper `traffic_step` (sampler's, counted for every Task of the crate) and `Stats::record`'s refused-request outcome, below |
| `part2.sampler` | 50 % (7 of 14) | 63 % (14 of 22) | 50 % (7 of 14) | `traffic_step`: a Job after the first finding no transfer ended (every one did), the peripheral found enabled at a first Job (no restart in the timing configuration) and the probe after a stop (never reached there; under stress, reached and never finding a transfer running, the R17.6 fix), a refused request (`tx && rx` false; none is refused in a measurement configuration); `Stats::record` and the body's `if accepted` false, the same refusal; under stress the loop-report closure of the injected spin (its Job ended by the Overrun_Response) and the first-Job outcomes of incarnations with one Job |

Every uncovered outcome is a refusal, a restart, or a backstop guard that the configuration does not drive by design (the isolation configuration drives the refusals, 11.7; the stress configuration the restarts), or the far side of a loop-report write in a Job the Overrun_Response ends. The inputs that would cover the reachable ones in a measurement configuration are a change to the configurations' purpose and stay an item of section 13; the threshold rises with them.

**The measurement campaign (R34.8).** Unattended: `cargo xtask nightly` runs the job, the hour-long release-timing and stress campaigns, and the two coverage runs; every run's raw data (the capture, the Kernel's records and mirror, the counters, the raw profile, the console) stays under `target/xtask-hil/<kind>/<variant>/run` and the reports and records under `docs/hil/` are the Evidence_Items the job reads (R33.4 keeps them keyed to the manifest's binaries). The margin stays 25 % (R34.6, 11.5). Task 19.2 is closed with this; what remains of the campaign is the hour-long runs under the scenario (the nightly) and the items above.

### 11.14 The conformance Application end to end: measured timing into the Analyzer, observed response times against its WCRTs (2026-11-06, task 19.4; R18.7, R29.5)

**The plain measurement configuration.** `nrf52840_measure` (`cargo xtask hil --plain`; the system crate's `plain-measure` feature: the plain declaration over 3 minutes with the measurement instrumentation of 11.13 and no injection; its `sampler` Budget is 600 µs against the plain demo's 500, since the first Job of the run starts the EasyDMA traffic scenario, 25,786 cycles measured, which with the margin passes 500 µs by 0.7 % and would have the analysis rejected under R28.4) gives the demo's records as declared: `docs/hil/kernel_timing_nrf52840_measure.json`, `wcet_nrf52840_measure.json`, the loop report, the coverage report. After the run the rig itself runs the Analyzer on them (with the cycle configurations' records the job's manifest lists, and this build among the manifest copy's HIL binaries, R33.4) and compares every Task's largest observed response time (the Kernel's record, Release due to Job end) with the WCRT computed: a step of the run, "observed response times against the measured WCRTs", which passes when every schedulable Task observed stays within its WCRT and the analysis is a PASS. The Analyzer's execution term stays the Budget (R29.2: the Kernel bounds a Job's execution by its Budget and the Overrun_Response), so the measured WCET_Records validate the Budgets under R28.4 and the measured Kernel_Timing_Parameters, blocking terms, and jitters set the WCRTs.

**Result, without injected Overruns (2026-11-06, `docs/hil/measure_nrf52840_measure.md`): PASS, every Task within.** The Analyzer on the measured records: PASS at 95 % utilization (J_rel 7,208 cycles observed, 9,010 with the margin; L_kernel 3,775 observed). Observed against computed:

| Task | Level | WCRT (ms) | Deadline (ms) | Observed max (ms) | Jobs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `part0.control` | A | 9.233 | 10 | 1.613 | 18,000 |
| `part0.echo` | A | 9.943 | 20 | 0.234 | 5,999 |
| `part0.sensor` | A | 13.249 | 20 | 6.769 | 8,999 |
| `part1.log` | C | 39.153 | 40 | 3.847 | 4,500 |
| `part1.pulse` | C | 14.159 | 15 | 6.056 | 9,999 |
| `part2.filter` | B | 16.484 | 25 | 7.007 | 7,200 |
| `part2.sampler` | B | 39.707 | 100 | 4.076 | 1,800 |

The observed maxima are a fraction of the WCRTs: the WCRTs charge every Task its whole Budget per Job (R29.2) and the blocking term of `control` and `echo` is `sensor`'s whole Critical_Section Budget, while the Jobs execute far less (the records) and the section holds for 6 ms. This is the schedulability claim under measured timing for the demo (P-13, P-17, P-18 as section 3 allocates them), made for the plain configuration: the job's item `analysis-rta-nrf52840_measure-measured` carries the analysis, and this run its confirmation on the board.

**With injected Overruns in the lower-criticality Partitions.** The stress configuration (11.4) is the case: part1's `log` and part2's `filter` and `sampler` overrun by design and the storm drives `pulse`'s source, while part0 (level A) must keep its WCRTs (R18.7). Its measured analysis had no fixed point (utilization past 100 % under the measured parameters: the configuration's Budgets were chosen for the fault injection), so `log`'s Budget is 4 ms there (6 in the plain demo; its Job measures 3.2 ms and its injected 20 ms spins overrun any Budget) and `sampler`'s 800 µs (the traffic start): at 95 % the fixed point exists and the analysis is a PASS. **Result (2026-11-06, `docs/hil/stress_nrf52840_stress_short.md`): every Task within, part0 with the lower Partitions overrunning throughout** (116 Overruns of `log`, 91 of `filter`, 92 restarts of part2 in the run, the source stormed):

| Task | Level | WCRT (ms) | Deadline (ms) | Observed max (ms) | Jobs completed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `part0.control` | A | 9.417 | 10 | 1.604 | 18,000 |
| `part0.echo` | A | 11.703 | 20 | 0.276 | 5,999 |
| `part0.sensor` | A | 13.507 | 20 | 6.804 | 8,999 |
| `part1.log` | C | 39.419 | 40 | 4.182 | 3,375 |
| `part1.pulse` | C | 14.458 | 15 | 6.854 | 11,951 |
| `part2.filter` | B | 17.151 | 25 | 7.416 | 6,300 |
| `part2.sampler` | B | 78.470 | 100 | 12.242 | 900 |

The lower Partitions' completed Jobs are those the Overrun_Response did not end; their observed responses are within too, as the Analyzer, which charges every Job its Budget, predicts for a configuration whose misbehaviour the Kernel bounds at the Budget (R18.7, R18.8). With the Budgets as they were, the stress configuration was outside the schedulability claim (11.5); with these it is inside it, and the job's `analysis-rta-nrf52840_stress_short-measured` item now has a verdict instead of an R28.4 rejection.

**The stress Budgets' headroom (2026-11-07).** The first campaign on the fixed Kernel (the fence, the deferral, the masked-span record) rejected the stress configuration's measured analysis twice under R28.4, both times at a Budget the record's 25 % margin had left no room under: `log`'s measured maximum rose from 204,523 to 205,569 cycles (0.5 %: the span record's two cycle-counter reads and one ICSR read per Kernel entry, charged to the interrupted Job as every entry is, and the run's own variance) and 255,654 became 256,961 against a Budget of 256,000; and in the hour-long run one `echo` Job of 122,000 ran 17,104 cycles (267 µs) against the previous campaign's maximum of 9,393 and a Budget of 19,200 (300 µs), the distribution otherwise as before (435 Jobs between 8,192 and 16,384 cycles against 360, the mean up 4.5 %). The one Job is unexplained in detail: the chained Kernel entries of a masked span landing on it (up to 26,123 cycles under the storm, charged to the interrupted Job until the next charge point) or the extra entries of a handled entry-window occurrence (four in that run, 5.8a) each suffice, and the record keeps the maximum, not the Job. The Budgets are now `log` 4.5 ms and `echo` 350 µs under the stress configuration (the margin's demand: 257,000 and 334 µs), the configuration's Generated_Config regenerated, and its records re-measured. **With L_kernel from the measured span the stress configuration is no longer a PASS:** the short run's analysis at 97 % utilization finds `log` (WCRT 40.277 ms against its 40 ms deadline) and `sampler` (100.220 against 100) not schedulable, while part0's Tasks and every other are schedulable and within their WCRTs on the board (`control` 1.677 of 9.464 ms, `sensor` 6.869 of 14.014, `pulse` 6.960 of 14.975), which is what R18.7 asks of the fault-injection configuration and what the step judges; the 0.11 ms blocking term of the single-entry L_kernel had made the two lowest Tasks' fixed points exist, the 0.4 ms one does not, and the configuration is not tuned further: its overload is its purpose. **The stress configuration's claim, as of 2026-11-07:** the Tasks of the highest Criticality_Level (part0) are schedulable under the measured parameters and within their WCRTs on the board; the lower-criticality Tasks are not schedulable and are not claimed to be (R18.7 protects part0 from them, which is what the configuration tests). The job's `analysis-rta-nrf52840_stress*-measured` items pass on that claim, naming the unschedulable Tasks, and fail should a part0 Task join them. **Which Tasks those are (2026-11-16).** The claim is by Criticality_Level and names no Task; the findings around it named `log` and `sampler`, the two that had no fixed point on 2026-11-07. Since the fourth pass `pulse` (part1, level C) is a third: 15.049 ms of 15 in its short run, 15.229 and 15.320 in the hour-long records, 15.256 in the short run of 2026-11-16, where the board's largest observed response of `pulse` is 5.912 ms over 11,947 Jobs. It is inside the claim as written (a lower-criticality Task of the overloaded configuration) and its Budget is left as it is: the configuration is not tuned towards a verdict for the Tasks it exists to overload, and the item still fails should a part0 Task lose its fixed point. Wherever this plan says "the same two Tasks" of a stress analysis before 2026-11-15, the set is those two; from that date it is three. The plain configuration remains a PASS under the same term (`control` 9.237 of 10 ms, `pulse` 14.617 of 15, `sampler` 79.177 of 100). The hour-long stress run on the raised Budgets (the same day): every rig step passing, one entry-window occurrence handled, the masked span 408.2 µs again, `echo`'s maximum 13,132 cycles over 122,000 Jobs (the 17,104 of the morning's run a one-off), and the analysis at 98 % utilization with the same two Tasks not schedulable and part0 within (`control` 1.679 of 9.474 ms, `echo` 0.336 of 11.831, `sensor` 6.906 of 14.026).

**What this closes and what it does not.** Task 19.4 is closed: the Analyzer takes measured WCETs (as Budget validation) and measured Kernel_Timing_Parameters, and the rig confirms the computed WCRTs on the board with and without injected Overruns. The claim rests on the inputs as recorded: the margin of 25 %, the instrumented build's figures (R34.7, 11.12), and the configurations' Budgets as declared for the measurement; the plain demo's QEMU Flight_Build keeps its placeholder analysis (section 1), the measurement configuration being the demo on the board. A Flight_Build measured without instrumentation stays in section 13.

### 11.15 Stack painting against the Link_Checker's bounds (2026-11-16, R37)

**The suite.** `cargo xtask hil --stack` (`rsk-hil::stack`; a step of `cargo xtask nightly` since the same day). The rig paints, the firmware does not: the binary is the short stress configuration's HIL build as every other suite measures it (`nrf52840_stress_short`: the Overruns, the restarts of part2, the interrupt storm, the EasyDMA traffic), with nothing added. After the flash, one probe command writes the run's pattern over every stack region the Link_Checker reports (the Kernel's MSP region from the end of its data to `_stack_start`, and each Partition's stack, the first `stack` bytes of its RAM region as the generated memory map places it) and resets the board in the same session (`probe-rs download --binary-format hex --reset` of one image holding the four regions). The Kernel's boot zeroes `.bss` and copies `.data` only, so a word keeps the pattern until a frame is written over it. After 165 s of the 180 s operating duration the regions are read back: the used depth of a region is the distance from its top to the lowest word without the pattern. The pattern's low half is a per-run nonce, so an earlier run's paint cannot stand in for this one's; a region with no word changed, or with its lowest word changed, fails; the run must have recorded Releases.

**Found on the way: the core runs between probe commands.** The first version painted each region with its own `probe-rs download` and read the regions back before a reset, on the reading that the flash leaves the core halted. It does not stay halted: DHCSR read 0x01010001 after a download (no S_HALT), and the region painted first had 152 bytes written by the Application by the time the fourth was painted. A paint spread over several commands is therefore a paint of a running system, over live frames. The suite paints and resets in one session for that reason; the read-back before the reset is gone with it, and the per-run pattern and the two failing cases above stand in its place.

**Result (`docs/hil/stack_nrf52840_stress_short.md`, binary `sha256:1ced71b2…c798b`): pass.**

| Region | Available (bytes) | Link_Checker bound | Used | Used of the bound |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| part0 | 4,096 | 1,896 | 152 | 8 % |
| part1 | 4,096 | 280 | 208 | 74 % |
| part2 | 4,096 | 1,960 | 288 | 15 % |
| Kernel (MSP) | 5,796 | 3,896 | 2,268 | 58 % |

37,171 periodic Releases had been recorded when the regions were read. A manual trial five seconds after a reset, before the suite was reworked, read the same Kernel figure (2,268 bytes) and part0's and part2's (152 and 288), and 144 bytes for part1: its deeper frames come later in the run.

**What the figure is and is not.** A lower bound on the deepest use of one run of one configuration: a frame may reserve words it never writes, a written word may equal the pattern, and the deepest path may not have run. The judgement is one-sided: a depth above the Link_Checker's bound would refute the bound, and none does; a depth below it does not prove it. part0's and part2's bounds are dominated by paths the run does not take or frames it does not fill (8 and 15 %); part1's 74 % and the Kernel's 58 % are the informative ones. Not done: the response and isolation configurations (they take the fault paths, whose Kernel frames these runs do not reach; their runs end in seconds and have another flow than the suite's), and the differential run against `cargo-call-stack` (8.5).

**Two further configurations, and the job's item (2026-11-16, later the same day).** `cargo xtask hil --stack --release-timing` and `--stack --plain` run the suite on the short release-timing and the measurement configurations, each under the stimulus it is measured with; both passed (`docs/hil/stack_nrf52840_timing_short.md`, `docs/hil/stack_nrf52840_measure.md`):

| Region | Timing, short: bound | used | Measurement: bound | used |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| part0 | 1,960 | 152 (8 %) | 2,088 | 280 (13 %) |
| part1 | 160 | 88 (55 %) | 256 | 184 (72 %) |
| part2 | 1,960 | 112 (6 %) | 2,056 | 208 (10 %) |
| Kernel (MSP) | 3,816 | 2,260 (59 %) | 3,928 | 2,268 (58 %) |

37,161 and 37,171 periodic Releases had been recorded at the reads. The Kernel's depth is the same to 8 bytes over the three configurations. The verify job now runs the suite on the short stress configuration as a step (`hil-stack-step`) and writes the Evidence_Item `hil-stack` (R37.2) with the report as its artifact, built in the job's sandbox and bound to the Build_Manifest like the other rig items; without the rig the step is skipped with a warning, as they are.

**The response and isolation configurations (2026-11-16, later; `cargo xtask hil --stack --response`, `--stack --isolation`).** The two suite configurations take the fault paths the measurement configurations do not: every response of R14.5 with the Endpoint injections, and the 71 refused accesses and EasyDMA requests of the isolation suite. Each is run from the reset for its suite's timeout with no host attached (the suite's own report over semihosting is not taken), and the run's report lists the Kernel's events by kind from its mirror, so that a run in which the cases did not happen is told from one in which they did. Both passed:

| Configuration | part0 used / bound | part1 | part2 | Kernel (MSP) | Events of the run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `nrf52840_response` (90 s) | 256 / 1,952 | 160 / 232 | 152 / 1,816 | 2,268 / 3,872 | 41: memmanage 1, deadline_miss 8, release_overlap 3, mit_violation 13, partition_restart 2, partition_stop 1, endpoint_full 10, message_rejected 1, safe_state 1 |
| `nrf52840_isolation` (60 s) | 312 / 3,124 | 192 / 2,184 | 256 / 3,188 | 2,260 / 3,872 | 177: memmanage 66, busfault 6, deadline_miss 6, release_overlap 1, mit_violation 13, dma_rejected 5, end_job 77, operating_end 1, safe_state 1 |

The Kernel's stack is at 2,260 to 2,268 bytes in all five configurations painted, fault paths or not: its high-water mark is set by a path every configuration takes. The largest share of a bound used is part1's in the response configuration, 160 of 232 bytes (69 %). Records `docs/hil/stack_nrf52840_response.md` and `stack_nrf52840_isolation.md`.

### HIL frequency (R50.1)

Once the HIL_Rig exists (task 7.1), the verification job runs the HIL suites of the table above at this frequency, each run starting with the read-back hash check (R48.4):

| When | Suites |
|---|---|
| Every change to the main branch that touches a Kernel crate, the Generator, a Target table, the linker script, or a toolchain pin | `hil-rig`, `hil-spike`, `hil-response`, `hil-boot-checks`, `hil-mpu-matrix` |
| Nightly, on the head of the main branch | Every suite |
| Before each Phase checkpoint and each release-candidate Flight_Build | Every suite, on the exact binary whose Build_Manifest the checkpoint cites |

Until 2026-11-01 no HIL suite ran; since the rig exists the job runs `hil-rig`, `hil-spike`, `hil-response`, since 2026-11-02 the isolation suite (`hil-mpu-matrix`, `hil-dma`, and the differential tests `hil-dt-hw-01` to `-14`, one run of about 30 seconds), and since 2026-11-04 the boot-check suite (`hil-boot-checks`, eleven runs of about 10 seconds) and the cycle-attribution test (`hil-cycles`, one run of about 12 seconds) on every run with the boards attached (and skips them with a warning otherwise); `hil-errata` is a review with its workaround checks inside the Link_Checker and the suites above; `hil-watchdog` is covered by the boot-check suite's watchdog case; the coverage runs of 11.13 (`cargo xtask hil --coverage [--stress]`, about four minutes each) are operator campaigns and nightly steps, as the short measurement runs are. Every suite of the table above now runs or is covered, the hour-long ones as operator campaigns, so R50.1 is met for the suites that exist (GAP-09, GAP-13 for the nightly schedule).

**The nightly campaign (2026-11-04; the coverage runs since 2026-11-06).** `cargo xtask nightly` runs the verification job, then the hour-long release-timing and stress campaigns and the two coverage runs of 11.13, one command after another with its own log, and writes `target/xtask-nightly/<date>/summary.md` (and `latest.md`); `xtask/launchd/com.ironoxide.nightly.plist` schedules it at 02:00 under `caffeinate` (install: copy to `~/Library/LaunchAgents` and `launchctl load -w`; the boards must be attached and the machine on AC power, 11.2). The agent is committed, not loaded: loading it is the bench operator's decision.

## 12. Criteria outside the allocation table (R43.5)

At requirement level, the criteria that no row of section 3 covers fall into four groups. The per-criterion list is `docs/traceability_matrix.md`, generated by `cargo xtask trace` (task 8.4, 2026-10-23) from requirements.md, design.md, tasks.md, this plan's section 3, the sources, and the evidence store: for every one of the 386 acceptance criteria, the allocation rows, design sections and Properties, tasks with their state, citing source files, and Evidence_Items with verdicts (R50.5); the criteria without an Evidence_Item grouped by Phase with the tasks that will produce it (R50.6); the criteria outside the allocation table with their Phase and tasks (R43.5); and the Profile's evidence names with their presence in the store. The verification job regenerates the matrix into the evidence store as an artifact of `check-evidence` and reports the count of criteria without an item. On 2026-10-23: 162 criteria had an Evidence_Item and 224 did not (164 of them of Phase P1 or P2, most with their task done); on 2026-11-05: 220 with an item and 166 without (110 of Phase P1 or P2), the HIL items and the measured analyses having added theirs; 78 criteria are in the allocation table and 308 outside it. The `covers` lists are derived, not hand-written: the Verus item's from the `Complete` column of section 9.5 (the plan is the single source of what the proofs are claimed to discharge; the earlier hand-written list had claimed 13 criteria this section marks partial, which the derivation removed), and the workspace-tests and Kani items' from the criteria the passing test functions and harnesses name in their names (`r28_5_...`) and doc comments (`R12.1`), 64 criteria from 62 tests on that date. A test that names a criterion is evidence for the part of it the test exercises; the matrix shows the item, the test's own text says what it covers. The remaining criteria without an item are mostly those whose tests do not yet name them, or whose evidence is a document or the hardware (section 11).

| Group | Requirements | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Documents, whose evidence is the document and its review | R1, R2, R4 (Profile; checked by `cargo xtask profile --check`); R43, R46.1, R46.2, R51, R52 (this plan); R49.2, R49.4, R49.6 (Hardware_Model text); R54 (Gap_Register); R61 (reuse record) | `check-static-checks`; review records |
| Build_System checks | R6.1 to R6.4, R33.3, R50, R55, R58, R59 | `check-*` items and the xtask fixtures |
| Phase P2 tools | R3, R15, R21 to R28, R30, R31, R33.1, R33.2 | Phase P2 |
| Later phases | R34 to R36, R53 (P3); R38 to R42 (P4); R5, R56, R60 (P5) | Their phases |

## 14. Structural coverage (R53; task 19.5, 2026-11-06)

**The evaluation (R53.1, ORQ-05).** Four ways of obtaining structural coverage of the Rust code were weighed against what the project can run today, on the host and on the Target, for the three levels of coverage the objectives need (MC/DC for DAL A, decision for DAL B, statement for DAL C):

| Method | What it gives | Host | Target | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| rustc's source-level instrumentation (`-C instrument-coverage`, LLVM's coverage mapping; `llvm-cov`) | statement (regions and lines) and, with `-Z coverage-options=branch`, the outcomes of every branch, which is decision coverage of `if`, `match`, `while`, and the short-circuit operators; `-Z coverage-options=condition` counts each condition of a boolean expression separately (its true and false outcomes, not MC/DC's independence pairs) | yes, from the test binaries | yes: the Partition crates in the coverage build (11.13), their counters read over the probe, the raw profile assembled on the host | selected; branch and condition options need the nightly of `toolchain/decision.toml [coverage]`, the pinned 1.95.0 gives regions and lines only |
| rustc's MC/DC instrumentation (`-Z coverage-options=mcdc`, LLVM's MC/DC bitmaps) | MC/DC at source level | - | - | not available: the option was removed from rustc in late 2025 and this nightly rejects it (`block`, `branch`, `condition` are what it offers); restoring it is an upstream 2026 goal (RN-40) |
| GNATcoverage (object- and source-level, documents Rust MC/DC, RN-40) | MC/DC, decision, statement, on the Target by trace or instrumentation | - | - | not evaluated here: a commercial tool outside the project's toolchain; the candidate for DAL A MC/DC when the applicant procures it, with Ferrocene (GAP-01) |
| object-level coverage from an instruction trace (ETM or the trace pins) | statement coverage of the object code, no source decisions | - | the DK's on-board J-Link has no ETM capture; the trace pins give the executing Task, not addresses | not possible on this rig |
| formal-analysis credit (DO-333, R52.4, section 9.4) | the objectives the proofs replace, not coverage figures | the Verus proofs of the logic (every exec path under the contracts) and the Kani harnesses (every path of the Kernel_Unsafe_Module's leaves under the bounds) | - | claimed as section 9.4 states (R53.4, below) |

**The selection.** Source-level instrumentation by rustc, measured on the Target for the Partition code (the coverage build of 11.13: levels A, B, and C are the three Partition crates, under the inputs of the measurement configurations and, as the suites gain coverage builds, the fault-injection ones) and on the host for the Kernel crates' tests; branch outcomes as the decision measure for DAL B, lines and regions as the statement measure for DAL C, and for DAL A the branch and condition outcomes with MC/DC recorded as open (GAP-02) until a tool provides it, the multi-condition decisions of the level A code being few enough to list: `control`'s one guard and `sensor`'s loop guards are `a && b` forms whose condition outcomes the `condition` option counts. The coverage toolchain is the nightly of `[coverage]`, a measurement aid like the coverage build; the Flight_Build's toolchain stays the pinned one (R53.1's record: tool rustc/llvm-cov of that nightly, source level, host for the Kernel and Target for the Partitions).

**The first reports (R53.3).** The job's step "Structural coverage" writes two kinds of Evidence_Item. `coverage-partitions-<variant>`: the committed on-target reports of 11.13 per crate (level A `p1_demo_part0`, level B `p1_demo_part2`, level C `p1_demo_part1`) and per Task entry against the recorded threshold (timing-short: control 100 %, echo 100 % with no branch, sensor 85 %, log 92 %, pulse 100 %, filter 50 %, sampler 50 %; stress-short: 100, 100, 83, 90, 100, 70, 63 %; the plain measurement configuration of 11.14: 100, 100, 83, 92, 100, 50, 50 %; the uncovered outcomes as 11.13 lists them). `coverage-kernel-host`: the Kernel crates' host tests (rsk-logic's property and unit tests, rsk-kernel's) under the same instrumentation, llvm-cov's totals over the Kernel sources: lines 7.0 % (267 of 3,798), branch outcomes 68.9 % (62 of 90 the test binaries carry), functions 9.2 % (30 of 327), regions 11.0 % (2026-11-06; `hw.rs` and `sim.rs`, the host doubles the tests drive, at 31 to 39 % of lines; the logic crate at 0 %, having no host test). The host figure is low and says so: the Kernel's requirements-based tests are the HIL suites on the Target and the Kani harnesses, neither of which the host instrumentation reaches, and its logic is proven rather than tested (section 9); the number measures what the host tests touch. **The Kernel on the Target (`coverage-kernel-target`, the same day).** The instrumented Kernel does not fit the Flight layout: its code is 125 KiB against the Kernel's flash (64 KiB until the same day, 128 KiB since: the HIL isolation build, with the differential-test probes, the trace, and the Δ_stamp probe, outgrew 64 KiB by 840 bytes in the day's last campaign, and the nRF52840 Target's Kernel flash was doubled; every layout, checksum, and record of the day is of the 128 KiB layout) and its 1,105 counters 8.8 KiB of RAM. The Target table therefore carries `nrf52840_coverage`, the same silicon with the Kernel's regions enlarged to 256 KiB of flash and 64 KiB of RAM (powers of two, as the layout's intervals are; the Partitions' regions follow), and the declaration variant `nrf52840_coverage` is the plain measurement configuration on it with every Budget two to three times the demo's (the instrumented Kernel's services, charged to the calling Job, run about three times slower: `control`'s 1 ms Budget overran at 1.24 ms). `cargo xtask hil --coverage --kernel` builds it with the Kernel crates (rsk-kernel, rsk-logic, rsk-entry, the system crate) instrumented beside the Partition crates, the Kernel's counters in `.kernel.cov` of its RAM (zeroed at instant 0 with the Partitions'); the functions Partition code runs inline from the Kernel crate (the SVC stubs, the register leaves, the state cells' accessors, the probes) carry `#[coverage(off)]` under the `coverage` feature, since their counters would be the Kernel's and unreachable under a Partition's view, the attribute's gate coming from the build's command line (`-Z crate-attr`) and never from the source (PR-36); the libcall of 11.13 is off too, or it would count itself. The run is the plain measurement run with its timing bounds reported and not judged (the instrumented Kernel is no timing evidence: J_rel 31,593 cycles there against 6,559, Δ_stamp 327 µs against 48.6). Result (2026-11-06, `docs/hil/coverage_kernel_nrf52840_coverage.json`, the job's `coverage-kernel-target`): **lines 68.6 % (3,017 of 4,400), branch outcomes 55.4 % (716 of 1,292), functions 75.5 % (280 of 371) of the Kernel sources** under the plain demo's inputs; what the plain run does not reach is the fault, restart, Overrun, violation, and boot-failure handling, which the response, stress, isolation, and boot-check suites exercise and which coverage builds of those configurations would add (section 13).

**The fault-injection suites' coverage (2026-11-07).** `cargo xtask hil --coverage --response` and `--coverage --isolation` run the response suite (11.4) and the isolation suite (11.7, 11.8) over the Partition crates instrumented as 11.13 describes, read the counters after the suite, and write `docs/hil/coverage_nrf52840_{response,isolation}.{md,json}`, which the job's step reads as `coverage-partitions-nrf52840_response` and `-nrf52840_isolation` beside the measurement configurations' items; the nightly runs both. The boot-check suite has no coverage build: it boots the Target once per mutated check and the counters are zeroed at each instant 0. Results: both suites pass under instrumentation with every step as on the plain build (the response suite's 29 events and every response as declared; the isolation suite's 75 refused attempts, 5 EasyDMA refusals, 14 Hardware_Model rules). Response: control 92 % (13 of 14 branch outcomes), echo 83 %, sensor 83 %, log 95 %, pulse 91 %, filter 83 %, sampler 70 %; isolation: control 87 % (7 of 8), echo 75 %, sensor 75 %, log 100 %, pulse 100 %, filter 72 %, sampler 66 %; none below the threshold. The outcomes the measurement configurations left at 50 % (`filter`'s and `sampler`'s refusals and restarts) are reached here, where the suites drive them; the branch counts differ between the configurations because each declaration variant compiles its own feature-gated bodies, so the figures are per configuration, as 11.13's are, and are read beside them.
