**Source-to-object traceability (R53.2).** Source-level coverage of DAL A code needs an analysis of the object code not directly traceable to source. The Link_Checker's scans of the Flight_Build are its basis (section 1, `analysis-stack-p1-demo`; task 12.3): every compiler-generated check is listed (the implicit panic kinds, a bounds check through 21 Kernel functions and a remainder by zero through 2, no explicit Kernel panic), every `core` and `compiler_builtins` symbol linked is mapped to a Core_Subset item (17, the `core::fmt` ones unreachable), the Generator's output is the generated sources the lint and the coverage see as source, and the naked stubs are the Kernel_Unsafe_Module's listed assembly (13, no cross-section branch). What the analysis does not yet do: match the coverage mapping's regions to the object code of each function (the inlined `rsk` API and the generated wrappers appear under their own source names, 11.13), and decide the object-code verification of GAP-01; both stay with GAP-01 and GAP-02.

**Formal-analysis credit (R53.4).** As section 9.4 records: for the Kernel's logic the Verus proofs replace the structural-coverage objectives' purpose (every path of the exec code is covered by the proof of its contracts, DO-333 FM.6.4.4) and the testing that remains is listed there; for the Kernel_Unsafe_Module the Kani harnesses cover every path of the leaves under the bounds of 6.3; for the Partition code no credit is claimed and the coverage above is the evidence. No objective is claimed replaced for the data and control coupling coverage (Annex A table A-7, objective 8), which stays with GAP-02.

- two observations of the first runs under the EasyDMA traffic scenario (11.13), each once and not reproduced: a wire answer of `pulse` 160 µs beyond its 15 ms deadline with no Kernel-side miss (explained 2026-11-07, 11.13: the masked span measured end to end reaches 293 µs, a timer entry that ends a Job, unwinds it, and tail-chains into the next dispatch, which L_kernel's single-entry basis of 85 µs did not bound; L_kernel is taken from the measured span since, 25,000 cycles exported), and the agent's largest `control` Job 7.9 % above the trace pin's longest run (the pins' 18,322 runs for 18,000 Jobs are to be read from the capture);
- the uncovered branch outcomes of the measurement inputs (11.13) are listed with their reasons; the inputs that would cover the reachable ones (a refused request in a measurement configuration, a restart in the timing configuration) are a change to the configurations to be weighed against their purpose;

- (closed 2026-11-08: the two open assumptions of the R41.3 proof; A-P7 is a Kernel rule, a rejected message re-initializes its Endpoint, and A-P8 a theorem over the declared gating, 5.15; sections 5.10 to 5.15 reviewed and signed off the same day); still standing with their reasons (5.15): that the Jobs gate their roles' operations only as `after` declares (an App_Prover obligation on the generated Job wrapper, or a lint on the bodies, would discharge it), that the bodies follow the projections (A-P1, narrowed to the rendering of the verified tables and finding 4), and, for A-P2, only that nothing but the Kernel's `copy_in` writes the Endpoint storage (R16.1; the role API's half is an App_Prover obligation since 2026-11-09, `rsk::channel`); the general theorem of R41.3 is proven for the two-role fragment (`theory::theorem_two_role_progress`, 5.15; every Endpoint of the Profile binds two roles) with its bridge to the flattened automaton that `compose.rs` takes; **the multiparty case with the full merge (three or more roles on one Endpoint, task 21.7) is proven since 2026-11-09 (`multiparty::theorem_multiparty_progress`, over the acceptance conditions `acceptn` of the syntax, 5.15);** the lexer verified too the same night (`lex_v`; `chars_of`, the standard library's decoding of the text into characters, is the one external body of the path); for two roles nothing else (the acceptance `accept_two_role`, the conversion `to_theory`, the flattening `flatten_two`, and the exploration `explore_system` are verified); for three or four roles nothing else either since later that day (`acceptn_exec`, `flatten_n`, and `explore_system` run by `check_protocol`; the plain-Rust `explore::certify` is a cross-check); (`log` has an App_Prover member since 2026-11-09, `proofs/creusot/part1`); its counterexample search (`tests/r41_3_search.rs`) found none in 100,000 generated types and its exhaustive enumeration (`tests/r41_3_enumeration.rs`) none in 1,305,724 small Protocols (5.15; a random run at depth 4 and capacity 8 exhausted the explorer's memory, so both run at capacity 2);
- (closed 2026-11-16: the HIL protocol-violation and rejected-message cases of tasks 21.3 and 21.4 are injected on the board by the response configuration and judged, 11.4; the peer-state step passes with them; Δ_send and Δ_recv measured by the campaign of the same day on the plain measurement configuration: 2,306 cycles observed over 89,104 sends, 2,883 with the margin, and 902 observed, 1,128 with the margin);
- (closed 2026-11-15 by argument, no Kernel change: the Analyzer's count of Endpoint operations per Job is the declaration's `ops` (5.14), checked by nothing at run time, and needs no check for R18.7. A Job's Endpoint calls take no charge point (`svc_core`: `number != SVC_JOB_END ==> final(state).last_switch == old(state).last_switch`, Verus, with the mutant `svc-lock-takes-a-charge-point`; 7.3, HW-13), so the Kernel's time in every such call is consumed from the calling Job's Budget: a Job that performs more operations than it declares reaches its Budget sooner and is answered by its Partition's Overrun_Response (R18.2, R18.3), and what it takes from other Tasks stays within E_j. Blocking does not depend on the count: each operation is one Kernel-level section, and a Task is blocked by at most one (R8.4). The `ops` × max(Δ_send, Δ_recv) charged in O_i on top of the Budget is therefore pessimism for a measured Budget that already contains the calls, kept as it is; a Kernel-side count would only name the misuse earlier than the Overrun does);
- the HIL findings without an explanation: one pulse unanswered in the first hour-long timing run (11.3); one UsageFault of `control` at a release instant in a short timing run of 2026-11-05 (**reproduced 2026-11-06 in the evening campaign's timing-short run, this time with its diagnostics:** CFSR 0x00040000, UFSR.INVPC, at 168,860.008 ms, a nominal instant of `control`; the trace ring shows a GPIOTE intake of `pulse`'s source 39 µs before the time-base entry, the dispatch of `control` on a fresh frame at the stack top 100 µs later, and the fault 12 µs after it, before any instruction of the Job's body traced; INVPC is the processor's exception-return integrity check, which with NONBASETHRDENA set (DD-01) fails on an invalid EXC_RETURN or an exception state inconsistent with the returning frame, so the suspect is the dispatch return of DD-01 in the coincidence of an intake, a timed release, and a dispatch within 150 µs; `docs/hil/findings/timing_short_usagefault_2026-11-07/` holds the report, the 512-entry trace, and the events; **the same campaign's hour-long timing run reproduced it a second time** at 2,232,060.008 ms with the identical trace pattern, an intake 40 µs before the tick, `..._hour_usagefault_2026-11-07/`; twice in one evening under the timing configuration, never in the plain, stress, or coverage runs; the Partition restarted as declared, the runs otherwise clean. **Diagnosed the same evening (5.8a):** the UsageFault handler of HIL builds now records the processor's exception state and the faulting frame, a third reproduction (the sixth 3-minute run of a loop, this time `pulse`'s dispatch 14 µs after `filter`'s) showed RETTOBASE clear in ICSR, `filter`'s dispatch vector active in NVIC_IABR0, and NONBASETHRDENA clear in CCR: a dispatch vector taken in another dispatch stub's return window had returned to Thread mode with that exception active. **Fixed 2026-11-07, in three steps (5.8a):** the first fix (the vector re-pending itself) was a livelock that the widened-window witness build exposed before it ever ran in a Flight stub; the return window is closed with FAULTMASK, which the exception return clears; and the records' refresh then showed the interleaving at the stub's entry instruction, which no mask can precede, so a vector taken there disables itself and is re-enabled, pending, by the preempted stub's own entry. **Closed 2026-11-07 (5.8a):** the witness build without the fence and the deferral reproduces the fault at will (340 fault-record entries in 61 s) and with them handles both widened windows (189 occurrences in 180 s, no fault); the campaign's runs on the fixed Kernel are clean (the records refreshed on it), and the rig's step "dispatch deferrals (5.8a)" reports the deferrals of every timing and stress run, each a handled occurrence), its CFSR unread (11.3; the timing and stress rigs now print the fault record); the two one-offs of the stress configuration (11.6: the completed `log` Job above its Budget recurred on 2026-11-05 at 986,501 cycles, twice with one value to the cycle, on the 33cb41c binary alone (bisected: absent on its parent's and on the next binary's, so a latent condition that the binary's timing alignment hits, 11.6); the trace had frozen and the rig misjudged it (fixed, and the mirror now stops at the freeze); the archived trace of that binary (`docs/hil/findings/`) showed a `log` Job of 23 ms wall time through six preemptions and a part2 restart charged 986,501 cycles with no Overrun, the cause being `budget_event`'s "Overrun recorded once" inference from the account, replaced by `Budget::reported` and proven (11.6); the one-off is closed as a defect found and fixed; part0's counters zeroed, not reproduced); the stress configuration's lower-criticality Tasks are not schedulable under the measured parameters: its documented claim since 2026-11-07 is the protected Partition's schedulability alone (11.14), judged by the job;
- (closed 2026-11-16: stack painting on the rig for the Link_Checker's bounds, 11.15: every region within its bound on the short stress, the short release-timing and the measurement configurations, and the job's Evidence_Item `hil-stack`; the response and isolation configurations are not done);

- the doc comments of tests that establish a criterion without naming it: 55 of the 109 tests that named none were given their criteria on 2026-10-24 (the remaining 54 are tests of helpers with no criterion behind them: tokenizers, decoders, hash vectors, parsers); the test and Kani items' `covers` lists are derived from these names since 2026-10-23;
- property-based tests now exist for Properties 12 to 15 (proptest, 2026-10-26: `rsk-gen`, `rsk-model`, `rsk-analyzer` test suites); the Analyzer's Properties 16 to 18 run under proptest as well since 2026-11-15 (`tests/properties.rs`, module `generated`: each property's case is one function of a list of draws, which proptest generates and shrinks, 256 cases each; the fixed-seed runs stay for the counts that show the cases are not vacuous);
- confirmation of the Gap_Register's Annex A applicability, independence, and DO-333 and DO-330 items against the standards' texts (OPEN-01, OPEN-02);
- a differential test of the TIMER compare rule under the Kernel's own path (11.8): `MIN_COMPARE_DISTANCE` is now the measured 16 ticks, and the driver's post-write check is the safeguard; a probe that measures the capture-to-write latency of `hw::timebase::arm` itself would close ASM-19 entirely;
- the subregion-disable and overlap rules of HW-09 and the late-arrival rule of HW-14 have no silicon test (11.8, dispositions recorded); a layout that uses SRD bits would exercise the former;
- L_kernel is measured (the longest masked span end to end plus Δ_entry since 2026-11-07, 11.13; the longest single Kernel entry before), not statically bounded in cycles: the Profile_Lint's loop-bound report gives every Kernel loop's iteration bound (PR-16), but a static cycle bound needs an instruction-timing model of the Cortex-M4 with the flash's wait states, which the toolchain does not have (11.5);
- nRF52840 anomaly 155 (a GPIOTE IN event twice on edges closer than 1.3 µs) is masked by the MIT policy, and the Generator could set the anomaly's register for sources that may meet the condition; an Application owning SPIM3 inherits anomalies 174, 193, 195, and 198;
- two one-offs of the stress configuration (11.6): a completed `log` Job of 13.8 ms under a 6 ms Budget (R18.2) and part0's counters zeroed mid-run as by an unlogged restart; neither reproduced with the diagnostics now armed (a trace freeze on a completion above the Budget with an execution histogram, and a freeze on any event of part0 by Partition);
- every hardware-pending item of section 11.
